*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*comment PORT VALEN -- RIVER TRANSIT
*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

*comment Port Valen is an open-world hub: a long random playthrough revisits a
*comment free-repeat menu (the Pier's observe menu, the Slip's stalls) far more than
*comment the default 1000-visit test cap allows. *looplimit only relaxes that cap; a

*label transit_start
*set hours_to_pass 2
*set minutes_to_pass 0
*set time_advance_call_id "port_valen_transit_1"
*gosub_scene calendar advance_time

Before the ropes come loose, a company clerk hurries along the Alderford wharves with a rolled muster parchment tucked under one arm. He shouts each assignment twice so the deck crews can hear him over the weir.

The four hulls are loaded by function. The Vanguard takes the lead barge. The Cadre, baggage, and wounded fill the second. The Scouts take the third, their bows wrapped against spray. Captain Vane's raven banner flies above the fourth beside the mounted officers. At every gangplank, the clerk repeats the orders: prisoners to the guild pens, ledgers to the Patrician Quarter, wounded to the Carrion infirmary, wagons to the compound above the Iron Wharves.

"Last clearance," Sergeant Varren says when he catches you looking. He taps the Black Sinks seal on the packet with one blunt finger. "Once this is delivered, we go home. Valen first. Whatever the Guild has waiting after that is Vane's problem."

The flotilla casts off on the flood tide. Four grain barges ride low under pikes, baggage carts, horses, and four hundred sellswords. The crews shove away while the last ropes are still being coiled. The quay drifts back, and the surge of the river rises beneath the timbers before anyone has finished shouting the final clearance.

Then the channel takes you.

Alderford's timber wharves fall behind the stern wake. The weir's roar fades into rushing rapids, creaking rope, and complaining hull-timber. Smoke hangs over the roofs. Watch fires burn orange where the road climbs away from the bank. Behind you, the old imperial weir still holds the flood, but guild crews work its bronze gates now, and the tolls go to merchants rather than soldiers in a crown's colors.

Ahead, the Grey River narrows between limestone walls streaked with weed and old flood-lines. The depths run fast and black beneath the low sky. A deckhand spits over the rail and points toward the gorge.

"Grain, timber, soldiers, prisoners," he says to a recruit near the mast. "The channel's carried all of it. Keep your footing and it might carry you too."

*if (squad = "vanguard")
  Sergeant Varren works the deck rail near the bow, counting hulls and checking lashings with the same flint-eyed thoroughness he brought to the causeway. "River's not much different from a shield wall," he grunts as you pass. "Hold your footing, watch the man beside you, and don't be the fool who goes over the rail."
*if (squad = "scouts")
  Kestrel crouches low near the gunwale, watching the gorge walls slide past with the same unhurried attention she gave the marsh reeds. "Running stream reads the same as ground, if you know how to look at it," she says without turning. "Watch the current, not just where it's taking you."
*if (squad = "cadre")
  Ysolde has claimed a dry corner of the lead barge's cargo tent, river charts spread over her knees despite the spray. She glances up as you settle nearby. "I'd rather be wrong about a river than a battlefield," she says. "Rivers, at least, hold still long enough to be studied properly."

*goto transit_boom_approach

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

*label transit_boom_cleared
*if (vane_standing > 0)
  A skipper's whistle sounds twice from the lead barge, short and businesslike. Captain Vane's sappers marked the channel with a floating cork line the night before, and the barge crews thread it without a wasted word: four hundred soldiers moving down the channel like it's nothing more than a drill line.
*else
  A skipper's whistle sounds twice from the lead barge, short and businesslike. Word passes hull to hull that the channel ahead is clear all the way to the gorge mouth: Ysolde's river charting, paying off exactly the way she promised it would. The barges hold formation and glide through without so much as a scraped keel.

You lean on the rail and watch the grey river slide by, undisturbed by whatever used to wait beneath it.
*goto transit_boom_resolved

*comment Only this path forces a real page boundary. Reaching here means
*comment nothing has paused since transit_start's own advance_time call --

*comment double-advance the clock on a refresh (calendar.txt's lock only

*label transit_boom_hazard
*set codex_imperial_booms true
Word passes from hull to hull: nobody ever cleared the old imperial boom. It was built to close the river against raiders. Now the chain lies somewhere beneath the trade route, and four loaded barges are heading straight over it.

You cannot see the chain. You can see what the current does around it: a flat patch in the chop, a change in the ripples, then nothing. The lead barge's skipper has her tiller, a boat-pole, and only a few breaths to find the way through. Behind her, three more hulls follow the same line.

"Eyes on the channel!" the skipper shouts down the line, fighting the current with both hands on the tiller. "Anyone who's ever read river or reef, I want you at the rail *now*!"

*temp hint_boom_str ""
*temp hint_boom_dex ""
*temp hint_boom_wis ""
*if (show_stat_hints)
  *if (guidance_active)
    *set hint_boom_str " [Guidance: STR Check (DC 12) + 1d4]"
    *set hint_boom_dex " [Guidance: DEX Check (DC 11) + 1d4]"
    *set hint_boom_wis " [Guidance: WIS Check (DC 13) + 1d4]"
  *else
    *set hint_boom_str " [STR Check (DC 12)]"
    *set hint_boom_dex " [DEX Check (DC 11)]"
    *set hint_boom_wis " [WIS Check (DC 13)]"

*comment Weather-aware checks: same advantage/disadvantage plumbing every
*comment check already reads, just gated on *if (weather = "X") instead of a
*comment prep flag or racial trait -- no new engine mechanism. Physical
*comment (STR/DEX) checks care about a slick, violent deck; the visual (WIS)
*comment check cares about being able to see the current at all.
*temp weather_hazard_physical false
*temp weather_hazard_visual false
*if ((weather_wet) or (weather_cold))
  *set weather_hazard_physical true
*if ((weather_visibility = "poor") or (weather = "Snow"))
  *set weather_hazard_visual true

*choice
  *if ((known_guidance) and (not(guidance_active)))
    # [Cantrip: Guidance] Murmur the focusing cadence before the barge closes on the hazard.
      *set guidance_active true
      You whisper the soft focusing syllable, feeling a cool clarity steady your grip on the rail.
      *goto transit_boom_hazard

  # Grab a boat-pole and brace to shove off the submerged boom the instant the hull grinds close.${hint_boom_str}
    *if (weather_hazard_physical)
      *set disadvantage true
    *gosub_scene dice_engine roll_skill_check 12 "athletics" "" "" "" "" ""
    *if (check_success)
      You plant the pole against something hard and unyielding a foot below the surface, throwing your whole weight into the shove. The hull scrapes past with a groan of strained timber but no crack. The barge slides clear into midstream.
    *else
      You get the pole down late, and the sunken beam catches the ash timber with bone-jarring violence, wrenching your shoulder and tearing the wood from your grip. The barge grinds over the obstruction with a sickening shudder that throws icy spray over the deck and soaks half the baggage lashings.
      *set river_crossing_rattled true
    *goto transit_cold_approach

  # Watch the surface for the telltale flatness where the current breaks over something old and hidden, and call the line.${hint_boom_wis}
    *if (weather_hazard_visual)
      *set disadvantage true
    *gosub_scene dice_engine roll_skill_check 13 "survival" "" "" "" "" ""
    *if (check_success)
      You catch it: a strange, glassy flatness in the chop, thirty feet off the bow, exactly where a drowned mass of timber and chain would flatten the current above it. "PORT SIDE, NOW!" The skipper hauls the tiller on your word alone, and the barge slides past with yards to spare.
    *else
      You call the line a half-second too late, mistaking a harmless eddy for the deep channel. The skipper hauls hard on the tiller, but the swing carries the keel straight over the drowned boom. Rusted metal teeth bite the oak bottom with a deafening scrape, throwing you hard against the bulwark as spray sweeps the deck.
      *set river_crossing_rattled true
    *goto transit_cold_approach

  # Work along the gunwale to the bow, relaying the skipper's corrections down the line of poling crew before the current can outrun them.${hint_boom_dex}
    *if (weather_hazard_physical)
      *set disadvantage true
    *gosub_scene dice_engine roll_skill_check 11 "acrobatics" "" "" "" "" ""
    *if (check_success)
      You move hand over hand along the slick gunwale, catching each of the skipper's clipped corrections and passing them down the poling crew half a beat before they'd need them anyway. The barge threads the hazard in one smooth, unbroken motion.
    *else
      You lose your footing on the slick planking halfway down the line, catching yourself on a rope lashing just as the bow swings wide. Without your relay, the poling crew reacts a beat too late, and the barge strikes the submerged boom with a jarring heave that sends cold river spray sloshing over your boots.
      *set river_crossing_rattled true
    *goto transit_cold_approach

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*comment BEAT 2: COLD WATER / EXPOSURE
*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

*label transit_cold_approach
*set hours_to_pass 3
*set minutes_to_pass 0
*set time_advance_call_id "port_valen_transit_2"
*gosub_scene calendar advance_time

The gorge walls close in tighter through the afternoon, funneling a raw wind straight off the river and down the neck of every collar on deck. The four barges have lost their clean formation by then. One rides ahead around a bend, one falls behind, and the others drift close enough for shouted orders to cross the gap but too far apart for anyone to mistake the distance for safety.

Spray comes over the bow in sheets now, and the grey light gives no real warmth to dry by, just hours more of it, all the way to the gorge mouth. Men and women who spent the morning boasting over dice huddle under baggage tarps with their knees drawn up. Bowstrings are tucked beneath cloaks. Firewood is counted and recounted. A cook tries to coax a smokeless flame from damp chips while the river rolls past with the patience of something that has already outlasted every army sent along its banks.

Every mile down the gorge is paid in shivering muscles and frozen spray. The current drags four laden barges forward without pause, leaving no room to pull ashore, no ground to stamp feeling into your boots, and nothing to hold against the numbing draft but wet wool.

*if (river_crossing_rattled)
  Your gear is still soaked through from the boom crossing, and the cold has had all afternoon to work its way in past wet wool and numbed fingers.

*temp hint_cold_con ""
*temp hint_cold_wis ""
*temp hint_cold_dex ""
*if (show_stat_hints)
  *if (guidance_active)
    *set hint_cold_con " [Guidance: CON Check (DC 12) + 1d4]"
    *set hint_cold_wis " [Guidance: WIS Check (DC 11) + 1d4]"
    *set hint_cold_dex " [Guidance: DEX Check (DC 12) + 1d4]"
  *else
    *set hint_cold_con " [CON Check (DC 12)]"
    *set hint_cold_wis " [WIS Check (DC 11)]"
    *set hint_cold_dex " [DEX Check (DC 12)]"

*choice
  *if ((known_guidance) and (not(guidance_active)))
    # [Cantrip: Guidance] Murmur the focusing cadence against the cold and the ache in your shoulders.
      *set guidance_active true
      You whisper the soft focusing syllable, feeling a cool clarity settle the shiver out of your hands.
      *goto transit_cold_approach

  # Grit your teeth and absorb the cold directly, saving your strength for whatever's waiting at the far end.${hint_cold_con}
    *set check_stat "con"
    *set check_dc 12
    *set check_skill "Endure the Crossing"
    *if (river_crossing_rattled)
      *set disadvantage true
    *if (prep_waterproof_gear)
      *set advantage true
    *gosub_scene dice_engine roll_d20_check
    *if (check_success)
      You stop fighting the cold and just absorb it, hour after hour, until it stops being something you notice at all. By the time the wind eases, you're stiff but steady, still sharp behind the eyes.
    *else
      The cold settles deep into your chest, turning your breath ragged and your fingers numb as lead. By the time the wind eases, uncontrollable shivering has sapped the last warmth from your core.
      *set river_crossing_weary true
    *goto transit_arrival

  # Find the driest lee of the deck and manage your body heat deliberately instead of just enduring.${hint_cold_wis}
    *if (river_crossing_rattled)
      *set disadvantage true
    *if (prep_waterproof_gear)
      *set advantage true
    *gosub_scene dice_engine roll_skill_check 11 "survival" "" "" "" "" ""
    *if (check_success)
      You read the deck the way you'd read a campsite: where the wind cuts least, where the spray doesn't reach, where a huddle of baggage crates breaks the worst of it, and settle in there instead of white-knuckling the open rail.
    *else
      You misjudge the wind's shift twice in a row, and each wrong guess catches you in a fresh blast of icy spray that penetrates your woolens down to the skin.
      *set river_crossing_weary true
    *goto transit_arrival

  # Stay moving along the deck, working circulation back into numbed hands and feet rather than holding still against the cold.${hint_cold_dex}
    *set check_stat "dex"
    *set check_dc 12
    *set check_skill "Keep Moving"
    *if (river_crossing_rattled)
      *set disadvantage true
    *if (prep_waterproof_gear)
      *set advantage true
    *gosub_scene dice_engine roll_d20_check
    *if (check_success)
      You keep circulating along the crowded deck in short, steady loops, flexing numbed fingers and stamping feeling back into your boots before the cold can properly set in. It's not comfortable, but it works.
    *else
      You misjudge your footing on the slick planking and go down hard twice, barking both shins against a baggage chest and losing whatever heat your pacing had worked up.
      *set river_crossing_weary true
    *goto transit_arrival

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*comment ARRIVAL: PORT VALEN
*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

*label transit_arrival
*set codex_port_valen true
*set hours_to_pass 3
*set minutes_to_pass 0
*set time_advance_call_id "port_valen_transit_3"
*gosub_scene calendar advance_time

The gorge finally opens onto a wide tidal estuary, the Grey River's current fighting a rising sea-tide that pushes silt-laden floodwater back on itself in slow, churning eddies. The change is so sudden that the barges fall quiet: after hours of churning currents and sheer canyon walls, the horizon has room in it again, and beyond the last bend, the estuary stretches out to a hazy silver line that can only be the sea itself. Salt-wind comes off the bay carrying tar, rotting weed, and the rank, sweet stink of fish rendered down in market vats, undercut by something colder and cleaner than anything the gorge ever smelled like.

*if ((time_period = "Dusk") or (time_period = "Night"))
   Valen rises out of the darkening haze ahead: a sprawl of dark tile roofs, granite quays, and forested masts crowding an anchorage bigger than Alderford and the Black Sinks causeway put together. High-sided seagoing ships ride at anchor beyond the breakwater, their lanterns marking the channel, while flat-bottomed freight barges thread toward the inner wharves.
*else
   Valen rises through the thinning maritime haze ahead: a sprawl of weathered slate roofs, granite quays, and forested masts crowding an anchorage bigger than Alderford and the Black Sinks causeway put together. Tall-masted seagoing ships ride at anchor in the outer roads, sails brailed up tight, while flat-bottomed cargo barges thread between them toward the inner wharves.
Warehouse cranes turn above the waterfront slips. Guild flags snap from the harbor towers. Cargo moves under the changing light while clerks in dry coats stand above the mud and decide what every sack, blade, and body is worth.

Dressed limestone blocks show under the newer quay repairs, and a weather-eaten crown is still carved above the old gate arch. Below it, customs clerks in woolen mantles wave cargo through by ledger and seal while hired guards keep the line moving. A merchant's boy curses that the toll has changed hands again; a dockworker answers that the men with the scales decide what the crown forgot.

Gulls wheel and scream over the fish markets, riding the wind off the bay. Somewhere beyond the seawall, waves break against the granite barrier in a steady rhythm the upstream reaches never had.
*if (time_period = "Night")
  Quayside lanterns flicker to life along the seawall, and the watch bells mark a shift you cannot see through the dark. The tide surges in below, powerful and measured, marking not safety but arrival: the close of a long crossing, and the beginning of whatever Valen has paid the Carrion to do next.
*elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
  The harbor bell tolls the evening watch as the last usable light fades from the bay. The tide surges in below, powerful and measured, marking not safety but arrival: the close of a long crossing, and the beginning of whatever Valen has paid the Carrion to do next.
*else
  A bronze bell sounds from the customs tower, carrying cleanly across the basin. The tide surges in below, powerful and measured, marking not safety but arrival: the close of a long crossing, and the beginning of whatever Valen has paid the Carrion to do next.

At the pier head, a knot of Port Watch officers waits under a blue-and-brass municipal pennant. Their captain watches the barges unload, counting the black raven banners as they come ashore. When the last gangplank is secured, she walks straight past the guild clerks and stops before Captain Vane.

"The Watch has held the quay as ordered," she says. "The Patrician Quarter wants no looting, the Dredge-End wardens want no raids, and the council wants every street quiet and under order. Your company is to reinforce our patrols until further notice. No private collections. No unsanctioned arrests. No settling old debts under the cover of municipal colors."

Vane's gaze moves over the crowded waterfront, then over the ranks filing down from the barges. A veteran beside you gives a low whistle.

"Last time we marched out of Valen, there were two hundred of us," he mutters. "Look at us now. Twice the mouths, twice the steel, and the magistrates think they can fit us into a patrol roster."

The Watch captain offers Vane a sealed parchment writ bearing the Council mark. He breaks the wax, reads it once, and folds it into his coat.

"We report to the Watch at dawn," he says. "Tonight, we get inside our own walls."
*set codex_port_watch true

*if ((river_crossing_rattled) and (river_crossing_weary))
  You step ashore soaked, bruised, and running on fumes, every part of you aware of exactly how much worse this crossing could have gone, and how much of it you could have avoided.
*elseif (river_crossing_rattled)
  You reach the quayside still favoring a bruise from the boom crossing, but the worst of the cold never quite caught up with you.
*elseif (river_crossing_weary)
  You come off the gangplank dry-hulled but hollowed out by the cold, glad beyond words to feel solid ground underfoot again.
*else
  You make landfall clean and dry, the crossing behind you without a single mark to show for it.

*label port_valen_hub
*looplimit 100000
*set current_district "compound"
*gosub_scene calendar update_hub_condition
*comment Stable city recovery point: state has returned to the compound and its time/condition display is current.
*comment This hidden named checkpoint replaces the previous combat/town/dungeon checkpoint without touching manual saves.
*save_checkpoint autosafe_checkpoint
*temp pv_is_night false
*if ((time_period = "Night") or ((time_period = "Late Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn")))

[b]◈ THE CARRION COMPOUND[/b]
*line_break
[b][⏳ Port Valen Hour: ${clock_time} (${time_period}) | Hit Points: ${hp_current}/${hp_max}${hub_condition_str}][/b]

*temp pv_first_arrival false
*if (not(port_valen_hub_seen))
  *set port_valen_hub_seen true
  *set pv_first_arrival true
  The stout reinforced oak gates open onto the Carrion Company's walled garrison grounds: a sprawling compound of wooden barracks, packed mud, and pitched canvas crowded behind the city's seawall. New barracks crowd against old ones, and fresh bedrolls spill from the stores awning while quartermasters argue over space. Two recruits drag a soaked baggage chest past the drill lines while a woman in the company's black-and-red colors carries a bundle of bloodied bandages to the infirmary, shouting for boiled water over the clatter of wagon wheels. Near the watchtower, scout runners pass messages from hand to hand; one peels away toward the lower tide gate, another heading for the inland road.

  Captain Vane's voice carries from somewhere within the compound, drowned out by the forge-hammers. The words come in fragments: "…quarter the new intake…" then, "…Watch detail at dawn…" A moment later, Sergeant Varren appears on the catwalk and points three squads to separate sections of the grounds. The orders travel faster than the wagons. Men and women who have not yet found their bedrolls are already falling into place.

  *if (squad = "vanguard")
    The Vanguard has taken over the drill square. Sergeant Varren moves between two ranks of shield-bearers, thumping the rims of their bronze-rimmed shields when a grip slips or a strap hangs loose. Arming swords knock against bucklers as the soldiers turn together, the sound sharp and regular against the chime of the watchtower bells.

    Lyra sits on an overturned brine cask at the edge of the square, her recurve bow resting over her knees and a whetstone working along the edge of a narrow dagger. Her damp cloak is folded beside her, and her quiver lies open while she checks each black-fletched arrow for a cracked shaft.

    "You made it back with all your fingers," she says, looking up. "That puts you ahead of half the new intake. Varren's got us on wharf duty at dawn, so find somewhere dry before he decides wet boots are a discipline problem."

    A veteran calls for the next shield turn. Varren slams his palm against a buckler. "Together! If one of you leaves a gap, the whole shield wall pays for it!"
  *elseif (squad = "scouts")
    The Scout Company has spread itself through the spaces other soldiers leave empty. Kestrel stands in the lee of a wagon, her weather-stained cloak pinned close against the coastal wind, while bowhands string recurve bows and test the weight of their arrows. Short daggers hang in open sheaths at their hips, ready for work where a bow cannot be drawn.

    A pair of runners crouch over a charcoal map scratched on an upturned crate. One marks the quay with a burnt stick; the other traces the canal road toward the inland gates. Kestrel takes the map, folds it once, and sends them in opposite directions with a few quiet words.

    She notices you before you reach the wagon. "Routes and rotations," she says, tapping the folded map. "The Watch wants eyes on ground its own people don't cover. Rest while you can. You won't get much once the rotation starts."

    A bowstring snaps somewhere behind you. The scout holding it curses, checks the welt on her wrist, and reaches for a spare without waiting for help.
  *elseif (squad = "cadre")
    The Cadre has claimed a corner of the compound under the contract office, where canvas screens turn the worst of the wind. Ysolde sits at a trestle table in an ink-stained travelling coat, sorting navigation charts, wax tablets, and damp cargo slips into separate piles. Around her, staff-bearers in layered wool and leather cases work through small practical tasks: drying spell components, binding cracked satchels, and checking the brass fittings on their shod quarterstaffs.

    A cadre recruit with a dagger at each hip holds a lantern over a cargo manifest copied onto wet parchment. Another steadies a stack of account books with one hand while keeping the other near the hilt of a short dagger. Nobody speaks loudly. The scrape of Ysolde's brass stylus carries farther than the murmurs.

    Ysolde lifts her eyes when you approach. "Cargo manifests, customs tolls and the Watch's own books: all three keep disagreeing with each other," she says. "We'll make them agree before the week's out. Put your things somewhere the rain can't reach them."

    She rules a line down the parchment chart and sets a small brass weight on it. Outside the canvas, a runner calls for more lamp oil.
  *else
    *comment squad = "none" fallback
    Unattached recruits and teamsters crowd into the overflow bays beneath the wagon sheds. File corporals pace the mud with lanterns, assigning straw paillasses and calling roll from notched pine boards. For tonight, the company is confined to the garrison grounds under strict orders from Vane: hold quarters, keep off the city streets, and prepare for tomorrow's dawn muster.

  *if (not(vane_independent_operative))
    [i]Under Captain Vane's orders, the gates to the city are barred to company personnel tonight. You are confined to quarters until tomorrow's dawn muster with the Watch.[/i]
*else
  *comment ── LAYER 1: PLACE ──
  The iron-banded oak gates open onto the Carrion Company's walled garrison grounds: a sprawling compound of log barracks, packed mud, and pitched canvas crowded behind the city's seawall. The black raven banner snaps from the central watchtower above the stores awning. The air smells of coal smoke, damp wool, horse lather, and forge slag, and the packed earth is churned with wagon ruts and hobnailed boot prints.

  *comment ── LAYER 2: SKY & ATMOSPHERIC LIGHT ──
  *if (weather = "Rain")
    Rain beats steadily against the cedar roof shingles, turning the central enclosure into black mud that swallows boots to the ankle and filling the perimeter drainage ditches with churning brown runoff.
  *elseif (weather = "Storm")
    A driving gale howls off the bay, whipping the black raven banner into a frenzy and rattling the taut stores awnings against their pine posts.
  *elseif (weather = "Fog")
    Thick, salt-tinged sea-fog rolls through the open gateway, shrouding the watchtower and turning the mess fires into dull, haloed embers.
  *elseif ((weather = "Snow") or (weather = "Sleet"))
    Cold grey slush crusts the baggage wagons and stable rails, and sentries on the rampart keep their hands tucked deep into greased sheepskin mittens.
  *elseif (weather = "Blizzard")
    A freezing coastal blizzard scours the garrison with biting squalls, and the snow drives flat across the churned mud.
  *elseif (weather = "Clear")
    *if (pv_is_night)
      A cold moon stands over the estuary, silvering the wet shingles of the barracks roofs and the black line of the seawall.
    *elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
      The low sun throws the shadows of the parapet guards long across the mud, and the drying wagon ruts glow amber.
    *else
      Pale daylight breaks cleanly over the post, drying the wagon ruts and casting sharp silhouettes of the parapet guards over the muddy ground.
  *else
    *comment Overcast
    A leaden sky hangs low over the enclosure, trapping the sour tang of woodsmoke and wet leather between the barrack walls.

  *comment ── WEATHER MEMORY (calendar ground_state, strictly no people) ──
  *if ((ground_state = "sodden") and (not(weather_wet)))
    After days of rain, the central enclosure remains a morass of knee-deep red muck, the wagon ruts standing full of murky brown water.
  *elseif ((ground_state = "wet") and (not(weather_wet)))
    The yard mud is still thick and heavy from the recent rain, pulling at boot heels.

  *comment ── LAYER 3: STREET LIFE & GARRISON ACTIVITY ──
  *if ((weather_severity >= 3) and (not(pv_is_night)))
    The compound has battened down against the squall. Drill is suspended, wagons are lashed to foundation posts with heavy chain, and canvas awnings groan under the wind. Sentry reliefs on the breastwork move in double-strength with oilskin cowls drawn tight, while the off-duty ranks stay crowded inside the log barracks out of the gale.
    *if (day_of_week = "Marketday")
      Supply wagons from the inland road have stalled outside the gates, teamsters shouting through the storm as quartermasters argue over tarp-lines in the driving rain.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Tideday")
      High storm-surge hammers the seawall below the compound, the vibration shuddering faintly through the packed clay of the yard.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Forgeday")
      The forge sheds are shut fast against the gale, red hearth-smoke venting through chimney slats into the dark sky.
    *else
      Peat smoke hangs heavy beneath the barracks eaves, flattened by the wind.

  *elseif (pv_is_night)
    The compound is quiet beneath the night watch. Forged basket-braziers glow with red coke at the sally ports, casting long, wavering bars of iron light across the mud. Sentries in heavy wool mantles pace the catwalks in measured pairs, their polearms silhouetted against the dark sky, while the low hum of off-duty voices and the clatter of bone dice leak through the shuttered barrack slats.
    *if (squad = "vanguard")
      Near the gatehouse braziers, off-duty guards sit on overturned baggage chests scraping dried road-clay from their greaves before turning in.
    *if (squad = "scouts")
      Along the north catwalk, lookout reliefs stand in deep shadow along the parapet, watching the darkened cargo slips below.
    *if (squad = "cadre")
      High in the timber headquarters, lantern light burns steady behind oilskin transoms where night duty officers log the harbor watch rosters.

  *elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
    Dusk brings the return of the day's detachments. Armored squads trudge through the timber gate with mud-spattered boots and loosened sword-belts, lining up at the wash-troughs to rinse road-sweat from their necks. Blue smoke rolls thick from the cookhouse flues, and the yard fills with the smell of scorched mutton fat, boiled oats, and woodsmoke as the evening mess opens.

  *else
    *comment Daytime Active Garrison (Morning, Midday, Afternoon)
    The compound runs with the loud, disciplined friction of a company of mercenaries. Armorers hammer bent rivets over bench anvils, baggage carters grease dry wagon axles, and file corporals shout duty rosters across the packed clay.
    *if (day_of_week = "Marketday")
      Supply wagons crowd the outer gatehouse, teamsters hauling sacks of grain, wheels of hard cheese, and tubs of lard into the stores shed under the quartermaster's eye.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Tideday")
      *if (weather_wet)
        Wet-weather duty carries the barracks: off-duty soldiers mend damp harness gear beneath the covered porch, while the tide surges against the outer seawall.
      *else
        Lines of washed linen and damp woolens hang drying between the barracks stilts, snapping in the estuary breeze while the tide rises against the outer wall.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Forgeday")
      The repair smithies run at full heat: leather bellows groan, sparks shower the cinder floor, and the clang of sledges dressing pikes and re-shoeing pack mules rings across the enclosure.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Ironday")
      *if (weather_wet)
        Under the deep shelter of the armory shed, squads scrub mail shirts in sand-barrels and grease harness buckles away from the damp.
      *else
        The yard is lined with inspection gear: mail shirts scrubbed in sand-barrels, sharpened axes racked on timber trestles, and corporals checking harness buckles down the ranks.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Hearthday")
      Hearthday slows the yard's pace; maintenance details scrape harness grease by the woodpiles, while off-duty soldiers shelter under the porch eaves to share pipe-weed and hone knives on wet oilstones.
    *else
      *comment Greyday / Hallowday
      Routine maintenance carries the yard: pack mules are led to water, wood-details split cedar logs behind the stables, and runners carry dispatches between the squad rooms.

*if ((campaign_day - pv_mess_week_start_day) >= 7)
  *set pv_mess_week_start_day campaign_day
  *set pv_mess_rations_used 0
*if (vane_independent_operative) and (pv_mess_rations_used >= pv_mess_ration_cap)
  The quartermaster's stores for the week are already spoken for. Anyone still hungry will need coin for the waterfront taverns instead.

  *elseif (camp_vignette = 3)

  *else
    *if (weather_wet)
      [i]Under the overhang of the drill shed, two young recruits in padded arming doublets test stances with wooden practice hilts under the watchful eye of a veteran.[/i]
    *else
      [i]Two young recruits in padded arming doublets circle each other in the sawdust with wooden practice hilts, testing stances under the watchful eye of a scarred veteran.[/i]

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*comment TRAVEL: CITY-SCALE NAVIGATION

*comment localized place), so it never mixes both scales on one screen.

*label port_valen_travel
*looplimit 100000
[b]◈ PORT VALEN: CITY NAVIGATION[/b]
*line_break
The port city climbs from the tideline in uneven tiers of granite, timber, canal silt, and ancient limestone masonry.

*if (weather = "Rain")
  Steady coastal rain streams over the tiered roofs, rushing down stone gutters and carved drainage chutes toward the sea.
*elseif (weather = "Storm")
  A howling gale drives rain sideways through the road cuts, rattling iron trade-signs and churning the grey waters of the bay below.
*elseif (weather = "Fog")
  Thick sea-fog fills the low canal cuts and wharf slips, leaving the cathedral spires and upper terraces floating above a blind sea of mist.
*elseif ((weather = "Snow") or (weather = "Sleet"))
  Cold grey slush slicks the paved switchbacks, muffling the tread of boots and hooves along the stepped roads.
*elseif (weather = "Blizzard")
  A freezing gale sweeps the hillside, driving blinding snow across the deserted switchbacks and icing the stone stairs.
*elseif (weather = "Clear")
  *if ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    Cold starlight glints over the ascending tiers of the city, tracing dark gables and the black expanse of the anchorage below.
  *else
    Sharp daylight breaks across the terraces of the city, from the foam of the outer breakwater to the pale limestone cliffs above.
*else
  *comment Overcast
  *if ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    A starless, leaden sky hangs low over the rooftops, trapping the smoke of coal braziers and peat hearths between the buildings.
  *else
    A pewter sky hangs over the city terraces, casting a flat, cold gloom over the gables and cobbled switchbacks.

*comment Dynamic district vantages: what you observe from where you currently stand
*if (current_district = "compound")
  *if (weather_severity >= 3)
    From the Carrion compound's timber gates, the garrison yard is battened down against the storm. The palisade gates groan on their hinges, and only the catwalk sentries stand out against the gale as you look toward the city road.
  *elseif ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    From the garrison gates, the road descent is dark and quiet. Behind you, braziers cast long bars of orange light across silent log barracks, while sentries in heavy wool mantles watch the hillside approach.
  *elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
    From the compound threshold, the smell of woodsmoke and boiled oats drifts from the mess flues behind you. Returning duty squads unbuckle armor in the yard, their voices dying down as night falls over the roads below.
  *elseif (street_life = "thin")
    From the compound gates, the garrison works on beneath dripping canvas: armorers hammering in the forge sheds and sentries pacing the slick ramparts with cowls pulled tight.
  *else
    From the open timber gates, the company's busy enclosure lies behind you: wagon teams hauling stores through the mud, armorers clanking at their anvils, and the city spreading wide across the terraces below.

*elseif (current_district = "harbor")
  *if (weather_severity >= 3)
    From the Harbor Quayside, balance cranes stand chained fast to the wharves and the slips are deserted. Breakers smash over the seawall in sheets of white spray, and the distant wreck-bell tolls in frantic, broken clangs as you face the road inland.
  *elseif ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    From the edge of the cargo quays, dark sea slaps rhythmically against the stone retaining walls. Solitary mast lanterns bob out on the swell, while the warning bell on the shoals keeps its slow, lonely toll.
  *elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
    From the quayside plaza, porters are hauling the last freight bales into transit sheds while torch-runners light the iron braziers along the seawall, warm light spilling from waterfront taprooms.
  *elseif (street_life = "thin")
    From the harbor pavement, the cranes swing steadily beneath lashed tarpaulins, the creak of wet cables carrying through the damp over the ring of shipyard mauls.
  *else
    From the Harbor Quayside, the air is thick with salt spray and coal smoke. The clatter of iron-rimmed barrows and the shouting of draymen echo off the warehouse walls as traffic streams toward the inland roads.

*elseif (current_district = "civic_heights")
  *if (weather_severity >= 3)
    From the high limestone terraces of Civic Heights, the council plazas are swept bare by the gale. The great cathedral buttresses loom dark against the clouds, their chimes torn away by the wind.
  *elseif ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    From the upper civic esplanade, the grand council halls stand dark and silent behind iron-studded bronze grilles. Far below, the rest of the city descends in a quiet pattern of slate roofs and faint lantern points.
  *elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
    From Civic Heights, cathedral bells toll evening vespers across the district. Watch sentries pace the limestone balustrades, while clerks carrying leather dispatch cases hurry down the road stairs ahead of the curfew.
  *elseif (street_life = "thin")
    From the high terrace, petitioners huddle beneath the sheltered council arcades out of the rain, while guards in polished scale armor stand watchful in the recessed portals.
  *else
    From the broad stone esplanade of Civic Heights, the plaza hums with formal business: citizens waiting outside administrative offices, messengers taking the wide road stairs at a run, and priests in undyed wool moving between the cathedral porticos.

*elseif (current_district = "upper_wharves")
  *if (weather_severity >= 3)
    From the Patrician Quarter, the avenue is barred and silent. Heavy iron gates are locked across the great houses' courtyards, rainwater rushing clean over polished cobbles with not a soul in sight.
  *elseif ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    From the paved avenue, the great houses stand shut behind their estate walls and iron gates. Private gate guards with halberds and lanterns patrol the clean flagstones, their mastiffs growling at the shadows.
  *elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
    From the avenue, heavy oak doors are thudding shut along the great houses' courts. Liveried footmen light horn carriage lanterns along the curb as the day's callers pull away toward the upper manors.
  *elseif (street_life = "thin")
    From the Patrician Quarter, the street runs quiet. Private guards in matching surcoats shelter inside hooded stone sentry alcoves, watching the slick cobblestones with cold suspicion.
  *else
    From the Patrician Quarter, the avenue runs clean and stately. Enclosed carriages rattle over scrubbed stone, liveried gate guards flank the iron-studded gates of the great houses, and clerks in house colors hurry through arched courtyards.

*elseif (current_district = "dredge_end")
  *if (weather_severity >= 3)
    From the edge of Dredge-End, the lanes are shuttered and bare. Runoff sheets down the broken paving into the canal, the market awnings are lashed flat, and skiffs are hauled up with their keels lashed to the railings.
  *elseif ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    From the canal landing, the lanes below are pitch black. Unlit skiffs glide silently along the dark cuts, and quiet bird-whistles echo between the leaning walls as lookouts track the crossings.
  *elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
    From Dredge-End's canal ramp, the smell of peat smoke and frying fish rolls out from beneath the low eaves. Lanterns glow red behind bullseye glass along the leaning tenements, and crews crowd the lane mouth as the working boats tie off.
  *elseif (street_life = "thin")
    From the canal walkways, the lanes are half-empty under the rain. Puntmen pole slowly along the slick stone kerbs, while residents move in single file down the wet lanes with heads tucked into their collars.
  *else
    From Dredge-End, the narrow lanes are packed with rough traffic. Flat-bottomed punts jostle in the cuts, barrows of scrap iron and cured fish rattle over the broken paving, and the air hangs thick with river silt and coal smoke.

*elseif (current_district = "middle_ward")
  *if (weather_severity >= 3)
    From Conduit Square, the open terrace is battered by the gale. The bronze lion-head spouts are whipped into stinging spray across the empty fountain basin, bakehouse awnings are lashed flat, and every shop shutter is bolted against the weather as you face the switchback road.
  *elseif ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    From the central fountain, the square is hushed beneath the night curfew. The horn lanterns along Lantern Lane throw warm amber pools across the flagstones, and the hollow splash of the lion spouts echoes peacefully off the sleeping shopfronts.
  *elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
    From Conduit Square, the day's trade is packing away. Apprentices are bolting drop-shutters across the craft fronts, the sweet smell of caraway bread and hearth smoke drifts from Mother Marda's chimney, and forge hammers fall quiet down Smiths' Row as workers head toward the road stairs.
  *elseif (street_life = "thin")
    From the fountain crossroads, the ward works on under dripping eaves. Water pours from copper gutters into the street channels, carters shelter beneath the bakehouse awning, and steam rolls thick from the wash-house roofs into the damp air.
  *else
    From Conduit Square, the Middle Ward is alive with steady, unhurried craft work: carters pausing at the fountain to water draft horses, the crisp ring of brass files and forge hammers drifting from the side closes, and the aroma of baking rye hanging warm over the crossroads.

*comment Distance-based travel time, not a hand-authored point-to-point
*comment matrix: each district just needs its own distance from the
*comment compound (get_district_distance), and any leg's cost is the sum of
*comment both ends' distances -- see that label's own comment for why.
*set gdd_district current_district
*gosub get_district_distance
*temp pv_origin_dist gdd_distance
*temp pv_time_compound pv_origin_dist

*set gdd_district "harbor"
*gosub get_district_distance
*temp pv_time_harbor (pv_origin_dist + gdd_distance)
*set gdd_district "civic_heights"
*gosub get_district_distance
*temp pv_time_civic (pv_origin_dist + gdd_distance)
*set gdd_district "upper_wharves"
*gosub get_district_distance
*temp pv_time_upper (pv_origin_dist + gdd_distance)
*set gdd_district "dredge_end"
*gosub get_district_distance
*temp pv_time_dredge (pv_origin_dist + gdd_distance)
*set gdd_district "middle_ward"
*gosub get_district_distance
*temp pv_time_middle (pv_origin_dist + gdd_distance)

*comment BUTTON FORMAT (2026-09-26), same convention as the four district hub menus: destination first, bold,
*comment then the time. The bold names are the codex titles from web/mygame/lorebook-port-valen.js (The Carrion
*comment Compound, Harbor Quayside, Civic Heights, The Patrician Quarter, Dredge-End, The Middle Ward), so each one
*comment links to its own lorebook entry. Deliberately NO flavour tail here, unlike the district hubs: this is a travel
*comment menu, so the two things a player weighs are which place and how long, and the old verbs (Visit / Walk / Climb /
*comment Follow) only got in the way. Dropping them keeps every line at exactly its old rendered length, so nothing that
*comment fitted on one line starts wrapping. The "stay where you are" options keep a short tail, because there the tail
*comment is the only thing distinguishing them from the six travel options.
*choice
  *if (not(current_district = "compound"))
    # [b]The Carrion Compound[/b]. [~${pv_time_compound} min]
      *if (not(stat_bump_locked)) or (not(locked_stat_bump_page_id = page_id))
        *set stat_bump_locked true
        *set locked_stat_bump_page_id page_id
      *set pv_travel_dest "compound"
      *goto port_valen_travel_to
  *if (not(current_district = "harbor"))
    # [b]Harbor Quayside[/b]. [~${pv_time_harbor} min]
      *if (not(stat_bump_locked)) or (not(locked_stat_bump_page_id = page_id))

        *set locked_stat_bump_page_id page_id
      *set pv_travel_dest "harbor"

  *if (civic_heights_open) and (not(current_district = "civic_heights"))

  *if (upper_wharves_open) and (not(current_district = "upper_wharves"))

  *if (dredge_end_open) and (not(current_district = "dredge_end"))

  *if (middle_ward_open) and (not(current_district = "middle_ward"))
    # [b]The Middle Ward[/b]. [~${pv_time_middle} min]
      *if (not(stat_bump_locked)) or (not(locked_stat_bump_page_id = page_id))
        *set stat_bump_locked true

  *if (current_district = "compound")
    # [b]The Carrion Compound[/b]: stay inside. [0 min]
      *goto port_valen_hub
  *if (current_district = "harbor")
    # [b]Harbor Quayside[/b]: stay here. [0 min]
      *goto port_valen_harbor_pois
  *if (current_district = "civic_heights")
    # [b]Civic Heights[/b]: stay here. [0 min]
      *goto_scene port_valen/port_valen_civic_heights port_valen_civic_heights
  *if (current_district = "upper_wharves")
    # [b]The Patrician Quarter[/b]: stay here. [0 min]
      *goto_scene port_valen/port_valen_upper_wharves port_valen_upper_wharves
  *if (current_district = "dredge_end")
    # [b]Dredge-End[/b]: stay here. [0 min]
      *goto_scene port_valen/port_valen_dredge_end port_valen_dredge_end_arrive

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*comment SHARED TRAVEL PRIMITIVES
*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

*label get_district_distance
*comment Input: gdd_district (plain *create'd global, not *params -- see
*comment its own *create comment in startup.txt). Output: gdd_distance,
*comment in minutes from the compound (0). One number per location instead
*comment of one per pair -- a full point-to-point matrix for 5 locations is
*comment already 20 entries and only gets worse as the map grows.
*set gdd_distance 0
*if (gdd_district = "harbor")
  *set gdd_distance 15
*if (gdd_district = "civic_heights")
  *set gdd_distance 35
*if (gdd_district = "upper_wharves")
  *set gdd_distance 40
*if (gdd_district = "dredge_end")
  *set gdd_distance 60
*if (gdd_district = "middle_ward")
  *set gdd_distance 25
*return

*label port_valen_travel_to
*comment Reached via plain *goto (never *gosub -- see startup.txt's
*comment pv_travel_dest comment), with pv_travel_dest already set by the
*comment caller. Computes the leg's cost as origin-distance + destination-
*comment distance (compound's own distance is 0, so a compound<->district
*comment leg collapses to just that district's distance for free), advances
*comment the clock, updates current_district, then hands off to the
*comment destination's own label. Every transition -- opening travel from
*comment the compound AND each district's own "return to the compound" exit
*comment -- routes through here, so a leg costs the same either direction.
*if (current_district = pv_travel_dest)
  *temp ptt_total_minutes 0
*else
  *set gdd_district current_district
  *gosub get_district_distance
  *temp ptt_origin_dist gdd_distance
  *set gdd_district pv_travel_dest
  *gosub get_district_distance
  *temp ptt_total_minutes (ptt_origin_dist + gdd_distance)

*set current_district pv_travel_dest
*if (ptt_total_minutes > 0)
  *set hours_to_pass 0
  *set minutes_to_pass ptt_total_minutes
  *comment A leg between districts is outdoor time, so cold, heat and foul weather cost a
  *comment little extra hunger and fatigue (calendar.txt calc_exposure). The extra is tiny on
  *comment a short walk; the same two lines on a long march, camp or voyage are what matter.
  *set exposure_base_mins ptt_total_minutes
  *gosub_scene calendar calc_exposure
  *set time_advance_call_id "port_valen_travel_${page_id}"
  *gosub_scene calendar advance_time
  *gosub_scene calendar recalc_hp_from_neglect

  *if (hp_current <= 0)
    *set death_cause "starvation"
    *if (neglect_damage_fatigue > neglect_damage_hunger)
      *set death_cause "exhaustion"
    *goto_scene death death_screen

*comment Compound and Harbor stay same-file (*goto); Civic Heights, Upper
*comment Wharves, and Dredge-End were split into their own scene files (see
*comment each file's own header) once they had enough content to be worth
*comment isolating, so those three cross a file boundary via *goto_scene.
*if (pv_travel_dest = "compound")
  *goto port_valen_hub
*if (pv_travel_dest = "harbor")
  *goto port_valen_harbor
*if (pv_travel_dest = "civic_heights")
  *goto_scene port_valen/port_valen_civic_heights port_valen_civic_heights
*if (pv_travel_dest = "upper_wharves")
  *goto_scene port_valen/port_valen_upper_wharves port_valen_upper_wharves
*if (pv_travel_dest = "dredge_end")
  *goto_scene port_valen/port_valen_dredge_end port_valen_dredge_end_arrive
*if (pv_travel_dest = "middle_ward")
  *goto_scene port_valen/port_valen_middle_ward port_valen_middle_ward

[b]◈ HARBOR QUAYSIDE[/b]
*line_break
[b][⏳ Quayside Hour: ${clock_time} (${time_period}) | Hit Points: ${hp_current}/${hp_max}${hub_condition_str}][/b]
*line_break
*comment LAYER 1: PLACE (Harbor Quayside only, constant geography and smell)
The waterfront runs in one long line of stone and timber along the estuary. At its western end the fish market's granite ramp slopes into the basin beside the low wooden taverns. Beyond them the cargo quay runs east, its wooden slips and massive balance cranes jutting out over the swell. Midway along it stands a long hall of grey stone with a shingled roof, wide cart doors, and a great iron beam-scale hung from its gable over the quay. The quay ends at a squat customs tower where the road down from the terraces meets the quay gate. Past the tower the seawall turns to shipyard wall, and beyond that a long stone breakwater runs out toward the bar. Past the breakwater, deep-hulled merchant vessels ride at anchor in the outer roadstead, while closer to shore, flat-bottomed river barges and fishing smacks crowd the inner docks. The air is thick with salt spray, coal smoke, and drying cod.

*comment LAYER 2: SKY & WEATHER
*if (weather = "Rain")
  Steady rain sheets off the warehouse crane booms and drums against oiled canvas tarpaulins lashed over the freight stacks.
*elseif (weather = "Storm")
  A howling gale churns the outer roadstead into white froth. Foaming breakers smash against the sea breakwater, sending sheets of cold brine spray high over the slipways.
*elseif (weather = "Fog")
  Thick, brackish sea-fog blankets the inner basin, turning masthead lanterns into dim yellow halos and muffling the slow, mournful tolling of fog bells riding the channel buoys.
*elseif ((weather = "Snow") or (weather = "Sleet"))
  Freezing slush slicks the pier bollards and gangplanks, while smoke from charcoal braziers drifts low over the damp flagstones.
*elseif (weather = "Blizzard")
  A bitter squall drives freezing sea-spray and blinding snow across the piers, crusting mooring ropes in solid ice.
*elseif (weather = "Clear")
  *if ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    Cold starlight glints across the tidal basin, throwing the tangled rigging of merchant masts into sharp silhouette against the salt-scoured sky.
  *else
    Sharp daylight glints across the harbor basin, throwing the tangled rigging of merchant masts into sharp silhouette against the wind-scoured sky.
*else
  *comment Overcast
  *if ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    A murky, starless overcast hangs low over the river mouth, trapping the smell of coal smoke, salt-cured fish, and wet lumber above the waterline.
  *else
    A dull pewter sky hangs low over the bay mouth, trapping the smell of coal smoke, curing salt, and soaked logs across the shoreline.

*comment WEATHER MEMORY
*if ((ground_state = "sodden") and (not(weather_wet)))
  After days of rain, brown drainage runoff still cascades from the upper terraces, and pools of standing water choke the quayside gutters.
*elseif ((ground_state = "wet") and (not(weather_wet)))
  The granite flagstones and wooden slipways remain dark and slick from recent rain.

*comment LAYER 3: STREET LIFE (Strictly mutually exclusive time/weather states)
*if (weather_severity >= 3)
  The quayside has ground to an absolute halt. The balance cranes are chained fast to the granite piers, cargo hatches are battened down beneath heavy tarpaulins, and the warehouse doors are barred against the driving sea. Breakers crash over the lower slips in deafening sheets of white water. The only figures moving along the wharves are Port Watch sentries sheltering under the customs arches and hawser crews in streaming oilskins fighting to double the mooring lines before the surges snap them.
  *if (not((time_period = "Night") or ((time_period = "Pre-Dawn") or (time_period = "Dusk"))))
    *if (day_of_week = "Marketday")
      The quayside stalls stand boarded over and lashed with storm-lines; the fish auctions have been abandoned to the weather.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Tideday")
      The foaming storm-surge boils clear over the lower wharf steps, drowning the stone mooring bitts.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Hallowday")
      The little shrine at the foot of the breakwater is completely deserted, its votive alcove battered by sea-spray.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Forgeday")
      The shipyard hearths are banked dead and the pitch kettles stand cold on the slipways.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Greyday")
      The heavy coal scows and highland timber rafts are riding out the gale in the outer roads, none daring to risk the slips.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Ironday")
      The sail-lofts are shuttered tight and the drying racks have been stripped bare against the wind.
    *else
      The seawall taprooms have their storm-shutters bolted against the spray, thick peat smoke flattened against the roof-slates.

*elseif ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
  The warehouse rows stand locked and silent beneath the iron seawall lanterns. Along the darkened slips, heavy hulls creak against greased fenders with the heave of the tide. Out in the deep channel, the solitary lantern of a harbor patrol skiff rocks on the dark water, while the bronze wreck-bell out on the shoals tolls its hollow, measured warning into the salt mist.
  *if (day_of_week = "Marketday")
    The stone slips have been scrubbed clean of scales, and long lines of covered dray carts stand parked in the shadows, waiting for the morning tide to bring the inshore fleets.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Tideday")
    The midnight spring tide rides at its highest mark, black sea lapping level with the upper pier copings and lifting moored caravels high in their berths.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Hallowday")
    The waterfront is quiet under the midnight curfew, save for a solitary tallow votive flickering beneath the iron hood of the shrine at the foot of the breakwater.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Forgeday")
    The drydock smithies are dark, banked hearths radiating dull warmth into the drafty sheds while towering crane booms stand like gallows against the night sky.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Greyday")
    Laden coal barges ride low along the granite quays, night watchmen huddled over iron pot-braziers along the open decks.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Ironday")
    A stiff offshore breeze whistles through the bare rigging of the refit slips, halyards slapping rhythmically against spruce masts.
  *else
    *comment Hearthday
    The quayside taprooms burn later than usual, muffled sea-shanties and the scrape of stools leaking past felt draft-curtains into the cold night.

*elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
  The harbor is winding down with the falling light. Port watchmen unroll the heavy iron curfew chains across the customs gate, cargo padlocks snap shut along the transit sheds, and yellow candlelight spills through the fogged bullseye windows of The Cleaved Keel. Shifting crews trudge up from the slipways, boots ringing wet on the flagstones, while torch-runners move along the seawall lighting the peat braziers.
  *if (day_of_week = "Marketday")
    Porters hose down the granite fish-slips with buckets of sea water, sweeping crushed ice and offal into the tide while carters haul empty barrels toward the inland road.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Tideday")
    The dusk flood tide surges into the inner basin, and wharf crews hurry along the catwalks to slacken mooring lines before the hulls bind against the stone.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Hallowday")
    Distant vesper bells toll from Civic Heights above, and watermen gather in quiet knots along the landing stairs to share tobacco before the curfew horn.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Forgeday")
    Shipyard blacksmiths bank their forge fires with wet turf, the day's final showers of orange sparks dying out over the water as the hammer strokes cease.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Greyday")
    Lumber crews finish lashing the day's log rafts against the slipway pilings, double-checking the chain binders against the evening swell.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Ironday")
    Loft workers furl fresh bolts of sailcloth into protective tarpaulins, hoisting them into overhead racks before locking the workrooms.
  *else
    *comment Hearthday
    The waterfront empties early for Hearthday; the cranes stand deserted, and the docks settle into quiet rest well before the twilight fades.

*else
  *comment Daytime active harbor (Morning, Midday, Afternoon)
  *if (street_life = "normal")
    The quayside runs at full industrial roar. Massive balance cranes swing crates from hold to wharf on groaning chains, iron-rimmed barrows clatter violently across the granite flagstones, and customs clerks call out registration numbers over the din of shouting draymen. Gulls scream and circle the fish-slips, diving at discarded bait.
  *else
    The docks grind on through the weather. Cranes swing freight under lashed tarpaulins, barrows throw plumes of brown runoff across the wet stones, and hull clerks squint at damp daybooks from beneath greased leather cowls.
  *if (day_of_week = "Marketday")
    Marketday jams the lower slips with frantic trade: open-air fish auctions crowd the stone ramps, fishwives hawk salt cod over the roar of the basin, and heavy dray carts jockey for space outside the customs gate.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Tideday")
    The high flood tide brings deep-draft merchant vessels right alongside the granite quays; crews work at a dead run to discharge foreign freight and heave ballast before the water begins to shelf away.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Forgeday")
    The Iron Wharves operate at full blast: pitch cauldrons bubble black along the ways, steam roars from the plank-bending trenches, and the rhythmic clang of caulking irons and shipyard sledges echoes across the entire bay.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Hallowday")
    Commercial labor is subdued for the church calendar; fewer carts crowd the slips, wreaths of braided sea-grass bob against the pilings below the breakwater shrine, and crews work with muffled tools along the repair ways.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Greyday")
    Heavy bulk freight dominates the quayside: immense highland timber rafts and coal barges maneuver into the lower channels, deckhands poling through the mudbanks to guide the clumsy craft to the sawpit skids.
  *elseif (day_of_week = "Ironday")
    The working week begins in full force; sailmakers stretch long runs of flax canvas along the upper loft racks, riggers swarm the standing rigging of moored sea-vessels, and customs inspectors pace the newly docked tiers with measuring rods.
  *else
    *comment Hearthday
    A lighter tempo settles over the piers; commercial loading slows as families and off-duty watermen take to the quayside walkways, sharing tobacco and watching the outer swell.

*comment HOURLY ROTATING VIGNETTE (Runs only during fair daytime/dusk hours)
*if (not((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))) and (weather_severity < 3)
  *temp harbor_vignette (((campaign_day * 24) + clock_hour) modulo 6) + 1
  *if (harbor_vignette = 1)
    [i]A swarm of shrieking gulls dives violently over the stone coping as a fishmonger's boy heaves a bucket of crushed heads and guts into the dark tide.[/i]
  *elseif (harbor_vignette = 2)
    [i]Near the customs arch, a drayman hauls back hard on his lead lines, cursing furiously as an iron-rimmed barrel cart jams against a mooring bitt.[/i]
  *elseif (harbor_vignette = 3)
    [i]An immense wooden balance crane creaks directly overhead, its hemp cable groaning as a netted sling of cedar logs swings slowly out over the slipway.[/i]
  *elseif (harbor_vignette = 4)
    [i]Two foreign sailors in wool smocks argue with an open-air pie-seller beneath a canvas awning, waving unfamiliar copper coins in frustration.[/i]
  *elseif (harbor_vignette = 5)
    [i]A pair of Port Watch sentries in blue-and-brass surcoats stroll slowly past the slipway edge, their iron-banded cudgels tapping rhythmically against their boots.[/i]
  *else
    [i]Out on the water, a heavily laden river barge eases alongside the granite wharf, deckhands scrambling to drop heavy rope fenders before the hull strikes stone.[/i]

*comment SHORELINE, west to east: Fishmongers' Slip, Cleaved Keel, the Weigh House on the cargo quay, customs tower and
*comment quay gate (the road down from the terraces arrives here), Iron Wharves, breakwater and Pier. The hub stands on
*comment the open quay near the middle, so walking times follow the distance: the Weigh House and the tower are 5
*comment minutes away, the Keel and the Iron Wharves 10, the Slip and the Pier 15.
*comment BUTTON FORMAT (2026-09-26): destination first, bold, then the flavour -- [b]Place[/b]: what it is like.
*comment Six prose sentences all opening on a verb are hard to skim when you are only trying to work out where
*comment each one goes, so the place name now leads and the [~N min] suffix is the scannable right-hand column.
*comment Keep every line at or under the old longest (88 rendered chars, the Cargo Quay button) so nothing that
*comment fitted on one line before starts wrapping. The Weigh House is the one conditional name here (wh_named), and
*comment it closes the bold INSIDE each branch rather than around the whole @{...}: [b]@{cond Name[/b]|[/b]a
*comment description}: tail. The point is that bold marks a NAME. Before the player has read the lintel the place has
*comment no name, only a description, so the unnamed branch opens and closes bold immediately and renders nothing
*comment bold. Wrapping the whole conditional instead would bold the description too, which reads as though the
*comment player already had a name for it. Both spellings resolve and neither leaks a raw "@{"; this one is correct.
*comment NB the story-linker already turns an unlocked place name in here into a codex link, so a bolded name can
*comment render bold AND underlined once the player has been there; that is intended, not a styling bug.
*gosub_scene port_valen/radiant/radiant_hub rh_check
*choice
  # [b]Fishmongers' Slip[/b]: follow the smell of brine and woodsmoke. [~15 min]
    *goto pv_poi_fish_slip
  # [b]The Cleaved Keel[/b]: where the porter crews drink their wages. [~10 min]
    *goto pv_poi_tavern
  # [b]@{wh_named The Weigh House[/b]|[/b]The long grey hall under the iron beam-scale}: the city's scales. [~5 min]
    *set minutes_to_pass 5
    *set time_advance_call_id "pv_wh_1"
    *gosub pv_wh_walk_time
    *goto pv_wh_door
  # [b]Cargo Quay[/b]: walk to the customs tower, where the dockmaster logs every hull. [~5 min]
    *goto pv_poi_quays
  # [b]Iron Wharves[/b]: step down to the shipwrights' ways. [~10 min]
    *goto pv_poi_drydock
  # [b]The Pier[/b]: walk out the long pier to the wreck-bell over the shoals. [~15 min]
    *goto pv_poi_pier
  *if (rj_has_job)
    # [b]Your posting[/b]: read what the insignia holds.
      *set rh_from "harbor"
      *goto_scene port_valen/radiant/radiant_hub rh_hub
  # Move on to the rest of the city.
    *goto port_valen_travel

[b]◈ THE CLEAVED KEEL[/b]
*line_break

*if (not(pv_tavern_seen))
  *set pv_tavern_seen true
  The Cleaved Keel stands right where the fish market gives up and the quayside taverns take over, with the cranes of the cargo quay lining the seawall beyond it: a low wooden hall on pilings above the tideline, its sign a keel split clean down the middle by a painted shipwright's wedge.

  Inside, the air hits you with sprat smoke, wet wool, and stale beer. Behind the wide bar, a barrel-chested man with a salt-grey beard and a leather-strapped wooden stump below his left knee draws a pair of ales at once without even looking down.

  "Ale, stew, or looking for a dry bench?" he calls across the counter as the door swings shut behind you. He slides the tankards onto the pass-board and fixes a pair of weathered, clear eyes your way, wiping down the oak plank with a damp rag.

  "Name's Maret," he grunts, resting his weight onto his good leg. "Spent twenty winters hauling logs on the shipwright slips before a snapped crane cable took the lower leg, but I run an orderly house. Keep the peace and your coin on the wood, and you're as welcome as anyone. What can I get you?"
  *goto pv_poi_tavern_choices
*else
  *if ((time_period = "Morning") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    The taproom is quiet this early, smelling of fresh pine sawdust and woodsmoke. Maret is up behind the service counter, stirring a cauldron of barley porridge over the hearth coals while a few sleepy draymen nurse steaming mugs. He gives you a nod as you step inside.

    "Mornin'. Porridge and tea are ready if you're breakin' fast."
  *elseif ((time_period = "Midday") or (time_period = "Afternoon"))
    The taproom is loud with the midday shift change: wet-wooled porters and river carters crowding the benches, trenchers clattering as Maret ladles stew and slides salt mackerel along the serving boards with practiced ease.

    "Hungry for a warm meal?" Maret calls over the noise. "Plenty fresh off the hearth."
  *else
    The taproom is packed to the roofbeams, thick with peat smoke, shouting dockhands, and the steady rattle of bone dice from the back table. Maret draws a pair of dark porters at once without breaking rhythm, his wooden peg thumping the floorboards behind the taps.

    "Dry throat?" he asks with a nod. "What'll it be?"
  *goto pv_poi_tavern_choices

*comment Reached ONLY when stepping away from the bar, dice table, or rumors
*label pv_poi_tavern_menu
*temp keel_menu_roll 0
*rand keel_menu_roll 1 4

[b]◈ THE CLEAVED KEEL — TAPROOM[/b]
*line_break
*if (keel_menu_roll = 1)
  The low timber taproom hums around you, peat smoke drifts lazily toward the blackened ceiling beams, the waterway's swell knocking gently against the foundation pilings, and an easy murmur of conversation drifting between the cedar tables.
*elseif (keel_menu_roll = 2)
  The Keel's rough warmth settles over you, thick with the scent of roasted malt, dried pine shavings, and harbor salt. Near the hearth, banked coals radiate dry, crackling heat, offering quiet shelter from the coastal wind outside.
*elseif (keel_menu_roll = 3)
  The tavern's steady rhythm carries on, trenchers clattering softly across the scarred trestles, the faint, dry rattle of bone dice from a corner table, and the tang of dark ale drying on scrubbed oak.
*else
  The room holds an unhurried, comfortable cadence. Golden lantern light glints off pewter tankards on the pass-board, while the low hum of dockside voices washes over the wooden benches.

*label pv_poi_tavern_choices
*choice
  # Step up to the bar for a warm meal or drink.
    *goto pv_poi_tavern_bar
  # Take a stool at the porter-crew dice game.
    *goto pv_poi_tavern_dice
  # Pull up a bench and listen to the dockside talk.
    *goto pv_poi_tavern_rumors
  # Return to the quayside.
    *page_break Back to the quayside…
    *goto port_valen_harbor_pois

*label pv_poi_tavern_bar
*temp bar_approach_roll 0
*rand bar_approach_roll 1 3

You step up to the scarred oak counter beneath the swinging iron lantern. 
*if (bar_approach_roll = 1)
  Maret wipes a wet circle off the timber with a coarse flannel rag, sets his good hand flat on the wood, and nods once in inquiry.
*elseif (bar_approach_roll = 2)
  Maret settles his weight against his wooden peg with a quiet creak of leather straps, setting aside a pair of dripping pewter flagons as he looks your way.
*else
  Maret rests both palms on the pass-board, his clear, weathered eyes taking you in with the practiced patience of an old docker who never asks a stranger twice.

*label pv_poi_tavern_bar_loop

*label pv_poi_tavern_dice
*if (not(pv_dice_met))
  *set pv_dice_met true
  You take a stool at the long trestle by the hearth, where dock porters and a pair of off-duty Watchmen sit around a chalk circle with a cut-down drinking horn, its rim banded in brass. The man running the game is a scarred longshoreman with a missing finger, grease-stained cuffs, and a screeching laugh like gulls fighting over offal. 

  "Shake 'em, Cutter. Give the newcomer a throw!" an off-duty porter calls out, slapping a pair of coppers onto the wood.
  The table-master grins, shaking the horn so the carved pieces rattle like teeth. "Fresh face at the slips?" Cutter asks, eyeing your coin. "Standard stake is four bits, roll three pips to beat the high mark. Your copper's as good as any porter's."
*else
  *if (((time_period = "Morning") or (time_period = "Midday")) or (time_period = "Afternoon"))
    You pull up a stool at the long plank table. During the daylight shift, the room is quiet: most of the dockhands are on the cranes, and Cutter is resting against the brick hearth with an oilstone, honing a notched belt knife while nursing a mug.
    *if (keel_dice_bankroll > 0)
      He sets the oilstone down with a grin. "Gangs are working the slips till dusk, friend, but the markers don't care what hour it is. Standard stake for a toss."
    *else
      He turns his coin-purse out over the tabletop with a rueful shrug. A couple of buttons and a fish hook drop out. "Tapped out, friend. The porters took me apart. Find me on the next turn when I've refilled the pouch."
  *else
    You take your seat at the crowded game. Shouting porters and off-duty Watchmen press close around the chalk ring as Cutter knocks the horn against the wood.
    *if ((day_of_week = "Marketday") or (day_of_week = "Tideday"))
      A couple of foreign sailors from the outer roads have joined the circle, their thick copper pieces clinking against the wood.
    *if (keel_dice_bankroll > 0)
      "Rolling again? Four copper to ante, roll three cubes to beat the high mark."
    *else
      Cutter turns his coin-purse out over the table with a rueful shrug. A couple of buttons and a fish hook drop out. "Tapped out tonight. The watermen took me apart. Find me after the late turn."

        You tip the horn with a dockhand's lazy rhythm and turn the ivory out clean onto the circle, a high point, and everybody in the circle knows it before Cutter does.
        Cutter barks that gull-fight laugh of his and sweeps the pot over without ceremony. "Fast wrists. There's a waste. Should've been born a crane-man."
        [b][🎲 Dice Wager Won: +8 Copper Bits][/b]
      *else

        "Fortune doesn't love anybody. Not even the ones who pay on time."
      *if (wager_bankroll_busted)

*label pv_poi_tavern_rumors
You find a bench against the wall with your back to it, a watchful habit, and let the taproom wash over you. The waterfront talks.

*if ((rotten_rib_quest_stage = "unstarted") and ((ysolde_timber_briefed) and (not(pv_tavern_rumor_rotten_rib))))
  *set pv_tavern_rumor_rotten_rib true
  *set met_brant true
  Over by the rear hearth, a pair of sawyers talk over sour beer. The Gilded Scales are riding the shipwrights hard down on Brant's slipway, they say. The guild factor has the master builder under a brutal fine for every tide the cradle sits occupied, and the apprentices whisper that the spruce cleared through the gates has been weeping green sap instead of ringing true.

    A ship's carpenter turns his cup slowly on the bar. "They launched the green-ribbed merchantman off the yard anyway. Somebody came asking questions down there and got walked to the gate for it, so now nobody asks. I'll not sail on her, and I'll not say why."
  *else
    A yard drayman chuckles into his tankard. "Clerk Elric looked like he'd seen a ghost down in the sawpits. Rolled three beams of cured highland oak back to Brant's slipway with his own men, and hasn't said a word since."
  *page_break The talk moves on…
  *goto pv_poi_tavern_menu
*elseif ((bell_quest_stage = "unstarted") and (not(pv_tavern_rumor_bell)))
  *set pv_tavern_rumor_bell true
  *set bell_heard_story true
  A skiff hand with a salt-cracked lip tells the end of the bench about the night the bell went quiet. A grain barge, he says, feeling in through weather with her crew listening for it, and the bell never sounded. She broke her back on the reef before dawn. By first light the wool was gone from the striker, and the watchmen at the stairs had seen nothing. "Nobody was out on the stone that night," he says. "Nobody could have told you it wasn't ringing until she hit."
  *page_break The talk moves on…
  *goto pv_poi_tavern_menu
*elseif (((bell_quest_stage = "resolved") or (bell_quest_stage = "failed")) and (not(pv_tavern_rumor_bell_after)))
  *set pv_tavern_rumor_bell_after true
  *if (bell_resolution = "reported")
    A caulker with tar to the elbows shakes his head into his cup. "The bell went off at all hours the other night, no rhythm to it, like a man beating a pot. The watchmen came off the pier with a purse, they say, and there's a lantern on the stairs now."
  *elseif (bell_resolution = "pocketed")
    A porter lowers his voice. "The watchmen found the striker bare and the steps empty, and there's talk a purse went missing off the stone."
  *elseif (bell_resolution = "bloodied")
    *set codex_wreck_law true

  *elseif (bell_walked_away)

  *else
    *set codex_wreck_law true
    A drayman leans back on his stool. "A coaster broke on the outer reef. Somebody was seen up the frame trying to tear the wool off when she struck. By first light the salvage crews had stripped her, and there was nothing left for customs to count as wreck-goods."

  *goto pv_poi_tavern_menu

  *if (fish_resolution = "lawful")

  *elseif (fish_resolution = "pragmatic")
    A drayman shrugs. "Same three at the block, same low prices. There is a new fellow drinking with them these days, and the fishwives spit when he passes."
  *elseif (fish_resolution = "strategic")

  *else
