*label pv_poi_brant_slipway
*if ((campaign_day - rotten_rib_resolved_day) <= 2)
  Down on Brant's slipway, genuine highland oak ribs gleam under a dark coat of pine pitch. Brant stands by his workbench smoothing an oak beam with a curved draw-blade amid the screech of long saws. The apprentices drive the last wooden pegs into the hull planks as the merchant vessel is readied for launch.
*else
  Down on the building slip, the merchant ship has long since left her cradle for the open sea. In her place, Brant and his apprentices are framing a stout forty-foot river barge, and the air is rich with fresh cedar shavings and hot pitch.

When he spots you on the staging, the old shipwright lays down his blade and wipes his hands on his leather apron. A rare grin crinkles the corners of his eyes.

"Back again," he greets you with a nod of real craftsman's respect. "Good to have honest boots on the staging. What's on your mind?"

*label pv_poi_brant_slipway_menu
*choice
  # Ask how the slipway's work is coming along.
    *if ((campaign_day - rotten_rib_resolved_day) <= 2)
      "She'll take the flood true," Brant says, rapping his knuckles against the third rib. It gives back a clear, bell-like ring that hums through the oak. "No spruce rot, no water in the bilges. The apprentices are driving the last pegs now. Thirty people will see their home ports because of you."
    *else
      "The merchant ship cleared the harbor chain on her tide without taking an inch of water in her bilges," Brant says proudly, running a hand over the new barge keel. "Her master sent back word: dry cargo all the way to the outer banks. We're laying down this grain barge now, honest heartwood from stem to stern."
    *goto pv_poi_brant_slipway_menu
  # Ask what he's hearing along the quays and the river channel.
    Brant leans against his bench and drops his voice under the yard noise. "Elric's been quiet as a mouse up in that stilt office. Walks past my slipway with his eyes on the mud, and I haven't seen a stick of green spruce near Bay Four since. Whatever thumb you've put on him, it's holding."
    *goto pv_poi_brant_slipway_menu
  # Ask whether he knows any skippers who might need reliable help or a quiet passage downriver.
    Brant gives a knowing chuckle. "This slipway has seen forty years of river craft through her cradle. When you need a word put in with an independent barge master, the sort who haul quiet cargo downriver and don't ask the guild's leave, you come see me first. I know every honest skipper between here and the sea."
    *goto pv_poi_brant_slipway_menu
  # Take your leave and step back to the shipyard lane.
    *goto pv_poi_drydock_menu

*label pv_poi_pier

*comment The Muffled Bell opens from here (see quest/MUFFLED_BELL_PLAN.md, section 1.3). It needs
*comment the old wreck story (a Keel rumor or overheard skiff hands), a first visit already made
*comment (so the silence is noticeable), Night or Pre-Dawn, weather a ship cannot see through but
*comment not a storm, and then a percent chance (bell_hook_chance, tuned in startup.txt). The flags
*comment are fresh: advance_time above refreshed update_weather_flags. bell_hook replaces the
*comment normal arrival because that prose has the bell tolling on nearly every branch.
*temp bell_roll 0
*if ((bell_quest_stage = "unstarted") and (bell_heard_story and pv_pier_seen))
  *if ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    *if ((weather_severity < 3) and (not(weather_visibility = "clear")))
      *rand bell_roll 1 100
      *if (bell_roll <= bell_hook_chance)
        *goto bell_hook

*if (not(pv_pier_seen))
  *set pv_pier_seen true
  *set codex_port_valen true
  The stone breakwater starts beyond the shipyard wall, where a small iron-hooded shrine stands beside the landing stairs with a brass plate on its post reading [i]The Tide-Well[/i]. It runs far out past the harbor arm to where the outer bar bares its jagged teeth at low water. At the seaward head, a massive bronze bell hangs from a heavy timber gallows-frame over the surf: the wreck-bell, worked by the heave of the surge itself, tolling its slow count over the shoals where the reef has claimed hulls for as long as Port Valen has kept records.
  The bronze striker has no rope. No one rings it for the living.
  Watermen passing along the stone jetty touch the weathered frame post without breaking stride—an old habit, a quiet toll paid to whatever the reef keeps below. You do it too, after watching enough scarred hands lay flat against the oak to feel foolish doing otherwise.
*else
  You walk the long granite causeway to the jetty head and back, salt spray on your lips, the wreck-bell keeping its relentless count over the shoals.

*comment LAYER 2: SKY & LIGHT AT THE BREAKWATER
*if (weather_severity >= 3)
  Massive breakers slam against the seaward masonry, throwing walls of white brine thirty feet into the air. The gallows-frame shudders under the wind, the heavy bronze bell swinging violently and tolling in frantic, broken clangs over the boiling shoals. You have to grip the rusted iron guide-cable to keep your footing against the gale.
*elseif (weather = "Rain")
  Rain hammers the exposed causeway, streaming from the bell's pitted rim in steady cords and washing across the dark granite flagstones into the sea.
*elseif (weather = "Fog")
  The breakwater dissolves into dense coastal fog thirty paces out. In the blind white void, the warning chime rings slow and hollow, marking the unseen barrier reef.
*elseif ((weather = "Snow") or (weather = "Sleet"))
  Freezing spray and slush coat the flagstones in dangerous black ice. The bronze bell wears a thick crust of rime, each heavy swing shedding fine ice dust into the dark water.
*elseif (weather = "Clear")
  *if ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    Cold starlight glints off the open roadstead, picking out the pale line of foam breaking over the submerged rocks.
  *else
    Sharp daylight cuts across the shipping bar, laying bare the dark spine of the limestone reef beneath the pale green tide while gulls circle the gallows-frame.
*else
  *comment Overcast
  *if ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    A low, starless overcast hangs over the estuary mouth, the black swell heaving against the breakwater while the bell tolls into the murk.
  *else
    Pewter skies press flat against the horizon, the wreck-bell keeping its slow, measured rhythm over the grey swell.

*comment Weather memory at the exposed jetty
*if ((ground_state = "sodden") and (not(weather_wet)))
  After days of storm, seawater and rain still pool deep in the hollows of the flagstones, and the granite remains slick with washed-up sea weed.
*elseif ((ground_state = "wet") and (not(weather_wet)))
  The granite causeway stays dark and salt-slick from the recent rains.

*comment LAYER 3: BREAKWATER ROUTINES & TRAFFIC
*if ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
  *if (weather_severity >= 3)
    No small craft dare approach the outer reef in this fury. The jetty is completely deserted, its exposed flagstones washed clean by breakers while the bell shrieks in the gale.
  *else
    In the shadows beneath the jetty-head crane, cloaked figures linger near the landing ladders, watching low, unlit hulls navigate the channel markers after curfew.
    *if (day_of_week = "Tideday")
      High water conceals the jagged reef-crests, and watchful eyes track the dark silhouettes of small boats riding the deep fairway with muffled sweeps.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Marketday")
      Early inshore fishing smacks already hover near the breakwater mouth, holding station in the darkness while waiting for dawn clearance at the slips.
    *else
      The causeway is lonely and cold, the ocean's slow surge working the bronze striker in heavy, solitary beats against the silence.

*elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
  *if (weather_severity >= 3)
    The last linesmen have hauled their gear clear of the spray, and nobody lingers near the gallows-frame as twilight falls.
  *else
    As dusk drains from the bay, coastal watermen tie off skiffs in the sheltered lee of the jetty, hauling nets up the lower stone ladders. Most pause at the gallows-post to lay a hand against the seasoned wood before heading toward the quayside taverns.

*else
  *comment Daytime (Morning, Midday, Afternoon)
  *if (weather_severity >= 3)
    The causeway is swept clear of casual foot traffic; only harbor watchmen in greased sheepskins venture onto the stones to check the integrity of the outer mooring rings.
  *else
    Beneath the jetty-head crane, quiet watchers linger out of the wind, keeping steady count of every vessel that rounds the harbor arm. 
    *if (day_of_week = "Marketday")
      Inshore fishing skiffs crowd the sheltered lee of the breakwater, crews hauling dripping wicker baskets up the lower stone ladders to waiting market hand-carts.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Tideday")
      The deep flood tide brings tall merchantmen past the head; harbor pilots signal incoming ships through the fairway channel using signal flags from the crane platform.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Hallowday")
      A quiet solemnity marks the breakwater; watermen pausing at the gallows-frame linger longer than usual, touching the notched wood in silence before heading to the parish services.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Forgeday")
      Maintenance crews work along the causeway with iron bars and lead mallets, checking mooring bollards and clearing barnacles from the landing steps.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Greyday")
      Heavy timber rafts and coal lighters creep past the breakwater head under the guidance of small pilot skiffs, avoiding the shallow bar.
    *elseif (day_of_week = "Ironday")
      The working week opens with steady traffic: harbor menders tar the wooden catwalks and replace chafed rope fenders along the granite steps.
    *else
      *comment Hearthday
      A calm pace settles over the jetty; solitary watermen sit along the sheltered inner wall with hand-lines, watching the tide roll across the outer rocks.

    *comment Hourly rotating breakwater vignette
    *temp pier_vignette (((campaign_day * 24) + clock_hour) modulo 6) + 1
    *if (pier_vignette = 1)
      [i]A solitary cormorant perched atop the gallows-frame spreads its oily black wings to dry in the wind, ignoring the heavy toll of the bell beneath it.[/i]
    *elseif (pier_vignette = 2)
      [i]A passing boatman in tall sea-boots lays his palm flat against the notched oak gallows-post, murmuring a word under his breath before heading up the causeway.[/i]
    *elseif (pier_vignette = 3)
      [i]Out along the reef line, a sudden swell breaks across a submerged limestone tooth, throwing a plume of white spray high into the salt air.[/i]
    *elseif (pier_vignette = 4)
      [i]A pilot skiff shoots past the jetty head under a single reefed lugsail, its helmsman leaning far out over the gunwale to gauge the current.[/i]
    *elseif (pier_vignette = 5)
      [i]Down on the slippery lower steps, two boys with wicker baskets scrape mussels from the granite blocks, keeping one eye on the rising surge.[/i]
    *else
      [i]A sudden gust off the open sea whips salt spray across the flagstones, the bronze striker ringing with a sharp, resonant chime that carries far over the basin.[/i]

*comment World memory of The Muffled Bell. Timeless on purpose: nothing here goes false on a
*comment later day, so no campaign_day arithmetic.
*if (bell_quest_stage = "resolved")
  A length of grease-stiff cord still hangs from the iron cleat at the foot of the frame post, and nobody has taken it down.
  *if (bell_resolution = "reported")
    A hooded lantern hangs on a hook by the landing stairs now, lit through the night.
*elseif (bell_quest_stage = "failed")
  More names than the post has room for are cut at hand height on the seaward side, crowded shoulder to shoulder.

*goto pv_poi_pier_menu

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*comment THE PIER SUB-HUB
*comment pv_poi_pier (above) pays the 15-minute entry cost once, prints the
*comment arrival vignette, then lands on pv_poi_pier_menu. Every pier exit
*comment returns to the menu, so the entry cost is never re-paid.
*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

*label pv_poi_pier_menu
*comment Re-entry line: a pool of three pure-prose variants per state (see harbor_pick in
*comment port_valen_harbor_pois), then the weather line.
*temp pier_pick 0
*rand pier_pick 1 3
*if ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
  *if (pier_pick = 1)
    The bell keeps its slow count out past the last of the stone, and the water has drawn back far enough that you can hear it working at the reef. Behind you the harbor lamps are small and far, and the causeway is empty from here to the quay.
  *elseif (pier_pick = 2)
    Nothing stirs on the breakwater but the bell and the water. The surge sucks at the reef below the gallows-frame, the granite is cold under your palm, and the harbor lamps behind you have shrunk to a scatter of yellow points.
  *else
    Out here the night has no edges. The bell tolls from the frame at the head of the stone, a beat behind the surge, and somewhere beyond the shoals a hull creaks at anchor, too far off to hail.
*elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
  *if (pier_pick = 1)
    Skiff hands are tying off along the lower ladders, and the bell is the loudest thing left on the breakwater. The watermen who lay a hand on the frame post do it quickly now, already turning toward the lamps of the quay.
  *elseif (pier_pick = 2)
    The last skiffs slide in under the lee of the jetty and their crews scramble up the ladders with baskets and oars. One by one they touch the frame post on the way past, and the bell fills the space they leave.
  *else
    Light drains off the bar until the reef is only a darker stripe in the water. The watermen have gone up to the taverns, the ladders drip, and the bell counts the surge alone across the emptying breakwater.
*elseif (weather_severity >= 3)
  *if (pier_pick = 1)
    The bell is the only sound left on the breakwater, and the surge is working it in broken strokes. The frame shakes on its footings, the causeway behind you has vanished into the weather, and every gust leans on your shoulder.
  *elseif (pier_pick = 2)
    Nothing on the breakwater is meant to be out in this. The bell swings wildly on its frame, striking whenever the surge and the wind agree, and the stone underfoot hums with each breaker that breaks against the seaward wall.
  *else
    The wind has taken the breakwater for itself. The bell clangs in broken bursts, the guide-cable sings, and you are holding the frame post as much to stay on your feet as for any respect owed to the dead.
*else
  *if (pier_pick = 1)
    Gulls circle the gallows-frame, and the bell sounds thin against the daytime noise of the harbor. Watermen pass along the jetty on their errands, each touching the frame post without breaking stride, and the crane at the jetty head turns slowly over the stones.
  *elseif (pier_pick = 2)
    The bell tolls small and steady under the daytime racket of the harbor. Down on the lower ladders skiff hands haggle over a catch, gulls quarrel on the gallows-frame, and the whole breakwater smells of weed and wet stone.
  *else
    Daylight makes the breakwater ordinary. Watermen come and go along the jetty on their errands, the bell keeps up its slow count as if none of them were there, and it sounds thin against the noise of the quay.
*if (weather = "Rain")
  Rain runs off the bell's rim in threads.
*elseif (weather = "Fog")
  Fog has taken everything past the crane, and only the bell tells you the breakwater goes on.
*elseif ((weather = "Snow") or (weather = "Sleet"))
  Ice is forming in the cracks between the paving stones.
*elseif (weather_severity >= 3)
  Spray comes over the parapet in sheets, and nobody with a choice is out here.

*choice
  # Stand under the gallows-frame and watch the bar.
    *goto pv_poi_pier_observe
  *if (not(weather_severity >= 3))
    # Go down the lower stone ladders and watch the skiffs come and go.
      *goto pv_poi_pier_ladders
  # Head back along the causeway to the quayside.
    *page_break Back toward the docks…
    *goto port_valen_harbor_pois

*label pv_poi_pier_observe
*temp obs_pick 0
*rand obs_pick 1 3
*if (obs_pick = 1)
  You stand under the gallows-frame with your hand flat on the timber post. The wood is grooved with hundreds of shallow cuts: initials, dates, and notch marks carved at every height an arm could reach. Decades of salt spray and passing palms have polished the oldest scars down to smooth grey hollows.
*elseif (obs_pick = 2)
  You lean near the gallows-frame, feeling the low shudder in the upright beam every time the surge lifts the striker. The post carries no bronze plaque and no official roster. There is only the raw oak, seasoned by winter gales, scored with countless private tallies left by anyone who lost a hull or a kin to the outer shoals.
*else
  You take shelter beside the seaward post, where the breakwater ends and the open water begins. The wood beneath your fingers is rough with dried brine and pitted with knife cuts. Each mark represents an owner who never docked and a voyage that ended on the stones below.

*if (weather_severity >= 3)
  Surge thunders against the foundation blocks, throwing cold sheets of spray straight over the timbers. The frame groans under the wind, the bell swinging hard on its iron pin and ringing in broken, frantic bursts across the churned water.
*elseif (weather = "Fog")
  A wall of sea-fog wraps the jetty head, swallowing the rocks twenty paces out. The bell rings with a dull, hollow tone that seems to come from everywhere at once, warning of unseen shallows in the white void.
*elseif (weather = "Rain")
  Rain washes over the granite lip and streams down the carved grooves of the post in dark ribbons. The sound of the surf drowns out everything except the heavy, wet strike of the bronze chime.
*elseif ((weather = "Snow") or (weather = "Sleet"))
  Freezing slush crusts the windward side of the timber, filling the carved names with white ridges. Each stroke of the bell shakes a fine dust of ice crystals into the dark tide.
*elseif (weather = "Clear")
  *if ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    Cold starlight glints off the open water, picking out the pale line of breakers rolling across the reef.
  *elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
    The setting sun washes the shoals in copper light, throwing the long spine of the submerged rocks into stark relief against the incoming swell.
  *else
    Sharp daylight cuts through the sea mist, glinting off the bronze bell and revealing the pale green water where the shallows shelf off.
*else
  *comment Overcast
  *if ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
    A low, dark ceiling of cloud presses over the estuary, leaving the water black and quiet between the slow strokes of the striker.
  *else
    A pewter sky sits flat against the water, giving the sea a leaden, unhurried weight as the swell heaves against the outer bar.

*if ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
  Out past the last granite block, the reef ridge shows as a long black shadow in the pale foam, and the bell counts the surge against it in slow, measured intervals.
*elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
  As dusk settles, the line between deep water and shallow rock begins to blur, the reef visible only by the sudden curl of whitewater along its crest.
*else
  Out past the last stone, the reef shows clean beneath the surface: a dark, jagged tooth of limestone running parallel to the fairway, waiting for any hull that wanders off the channel.

*page_break Back to the breakwater…
*goto pv_poi_pier_menu

*label pv_poi_pier_ladders
*temp ladder_pick 0
*rand ladder_pick 1 3
*if (ladder_pick = 1)
  You climb down the slippery stone steps cut into the lee side of the breakwater. Down here, protected from the open rollers, the smell of coal tar, soaked hemp, and fish scales hangs heavy in the stagnant air trapped against the granite blocks.
*elseif (ladder_pick = 2)
  You take the iron ladder rungs down toward the landing stage. The stone below is coated in slippery green weed, and the estuary swell lifts and drops the moored skiffs with a steady, hollow slap of timber against heavy rope bumpers.
*else
  You make your way down the landing steps to the lower catwalks, where the water surges rhythmically through the pilings. Small river skiffs and coastal smacks jostle against one another on short painter lines, their pine thwarts wet with spray.

*if (weather = "Fog")
  Dense sea-fog drifts between the hulls, deadening the clatter of rowlocks and turning the lanterns on the upper wall into blurred halos.
*elseif (weather = "Rain")
  Rain trickles down the masonry joints and drums against overturned wicker baskets and canvas boat-covers.
*elseif ((weather = "Snow") or (weather = "Sleet"))
  A crust of grey slush cakes the wooden landings and gunwales, making every foothold on the weed-grown stone treacherous.
*elseif (weather_cold)
  The draft coming off the low water bites through wool, and frost outlines the hemp mooring lines coiled around the iron bitts.
*else
  Brine and dry salt crust the higher steps where the high-tide mark dried in the breeze.

*if ((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn"))
  The landing stage is dark and watchful. Most of the small craft lie tethered and empty, riding the tide in silence, while the few skiff hands still aboard work by the dim glow of horn lanterns hooded down to narrow slits. Low whispers and the creak of wet oars carry across the water, dying out whenever someone looks up toward the pier.
*elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
  The evening run is finishing at the landing. Watermen haul dripping nets onto the lower planks to dry, secure spare oars beneath their benches, and scrub fish scales from their thwart boards with stiff bristle brushes before securing their painters for the night.
*else
  *comment Daytime (Morning, Midday, Afternoon)
  The lower steps are busy with routine river maintenance: watermen bailing bilge water with wooden scoops, mending torn seine nets along the staging, and hauling crates of fresh bait or shellfish up the narrow steps to the market carts above.

*comment One of the two ways to hear the wreck story that opens The Muffled Bell (the other is
*comment the Keel rumor). Daylight and Dusk only: the ladders are watchful, not chatty, at night.
*if ((bell_quest_stage = "unstarted") and (not(bell_heard_story)))
  *if (not((time_period = "Night") or (time_period = "Pre-Dawn")))
    *set bell_heard_story true
    Two skiff hands are coiling line on the lower planks, arguing about the grain barge that broke on the reef. "The striker was stuffed," says one. The other spits into the water. "Stuffed by who? Nobody saw a thing." "That's what I mean."

*page_break Back up to the breakwater…
*goto pv_poi_pier_menu

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*comment THE MUFFLED BELL (the Pier, see quest/MUFFLED_BELL_PLAN.md)
*comment Opened by the gate in pv_poi_pier. Beat 1 bell_hook, Beat 2 bell_b2 (the climax roll: the
*comment ketch is saved or wrecked), Beat 3 bell_b3 (the wreckers on the ladder; a failed roll
*comment starts fight_wreckers in combat.txt), Beat 4 bell_b4 (the pouch). Nobody is named. The
*comment player never arrests, hauls or hands anyone over; the Watch arrives and takes over. A
*comment bribe buys not being noticed, and cannot cover a bell the whole harbor hears, which is
*comment why the Watch turns up on every success route. Only one advance_time per page, so each
*comment beat starts after a *page_break.
*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

*label bell_hook
You walk the causeway out toward the head of the stone, and you know the sound this place makes: one slow stroke behind every heave of the sea.
*if (weather = "Fog")
  Fog has swallowed the harbor lamps behind you and laid a lid on the sea ahead.
*elseif (weather = "Rain")
  Rain slants across the stone and smears every light on the water.
*elseif (weather = "Snow")
  Snow drifts sideways across the stone and hides the far end of the reef.
*else
  Sleet ticks against the timber and freezes in the notches of the post.
Tonight the sea heaves and no stroke follows.

Ahead, the frame stands black in the murk at the head of the breakwater, and the bronze bell hangs in it over the surf, swinging with every swell. Something is wrong with the striker. Each swing ends in a dull thud, like a fist in a pillow, and the note that should roll out over the reef never leaves the timber.

Below the head of the stone, a dull glow moves on the landing stage: a horn lantern with a rag stuffed in its shutter. A hooded figure is going down the ladder into a rowboat, where someone is already sitting on the oars. Neither of them looks up.

Then you see the lights. Two yellow points hang low in the haze beyond the reef, one behind the other, slow and steady. A ship's mast lanterns, feeling their way in. They are lined up with the pale stripe of foam where the reef breaks.

*comment lint-ok two-options: plain yes or no
*choice
  # Go out to the post and see what is wrong with the striker.
    *set bell_quest_stage "active"
    *goto bell_b2
  # Turn back along the causeway.
    *goto bell_turn_back

*label bell_turn_back
*set bell_quest_stage "failed"
*set bell_resolution "wrecked"
*set bell_walked_away true
*set hours_to_pass 0
*set minutes_to_pass 15
*set time_advance_call_id "bell_turn_back_time"
*gosub_scene calendar advance_time
*gosub_scene calendar recalc_hp_from_neglect

*if (hp_current <= 0)
  *set death_cause "starvation"
  *if (neglect_damage_fatigue > neglect_damage_hunger)
    *set death_cause "exhaustion"
  *goto_scene death death_screen

You put the frame behind you and walk the causeway back toward the quay. The lights out on the water do not stop. Somewhere past the halfway stone you hear a long, low grinding, like a keel dragged over gravel, and then a crack. Voices come after it, small across the water, and then the knock of oars. You keep walking until the stone under your boots is the quay. Behind you, long after it can matter, the bell finds its voice at last: one huge slow stroke, as if someone has climbed the frame and pulled the wool out.

*page_break Back toward the docks…
*goto port_valen_harbor_pois

*label bell_b2
*set hours_to_pass 0
*set minutes_to_pass 15
*set time_advance_call_id "bell_b2_time"
*gosub_scene calendar advance_time
*gosub_scene calendar recalc_hp_from_neglect

*if (hp_current <= 0)
  *set death_cause "starvation"
  *if (neglect_damage_fatigue > neglect_damage_hunger)
    *set death_cause "exhaustion"
  *goto_scene death death_screen

You lay a hand on the frame post the way the watermen do. The timber is slick, and your fingers come away greasy and stinking of old lard. The wool is packed into the striker's head in a wad the size of a loaf, and a cord holds it there, run down the post to an iron cleat at its foot. Under the wad the striker swings and thuds. Out on the water the mast lanterns have not turned. You have a few minutes, and the bell has to ring.

*label bell_b2_hub
*temp hint_bell_str ""
*temp hint_bell_dex ""
*temp hint_bell_int ""
*if (show_stat_hints)
  *if (guidance_active)
    *set hint_bell_str " [STR DC 12 (+1d4 Guidance)]"
    *set hint_bell_dex " [DEX DC 12 (+1d4 Guidance)]"
    *set hint_bell_int " [INT DC 12 (+1d4 Guidance)]"
  *else
    *set hint_bell_str " [STR DC 12]"
    *set hint_bell_dex " [DEX DC 12]"
    *set hint_bell_int " [INT DC 12]"
  *if (weapon_type = "ranged")
    *set hint_bell_dex (hint_bell_dex & " (Advantage)")

*choice
  *if ((known_guidance) and (not(guidance_active)))
    # [Cantrip: Guidance] Murmur the focusing verse under the noise of the surf.
      *set guidance_active true
      You breathe the verse out through your teeth, and the shake goes out of your hands.
      *goto bell_b2_hub
  # Climb the frame and tear the wool off the striker by hand.${hint_bell_str}
    *gosub_scene dice_engine roll_skill_check 12 "athletics" "" "" "" "" ""
    *if (check_success)
      The timber is slick with lard, but the notches in the post are cut at every height of a hand, and you climb them like rungs. Halfway up you hook one arm round the beam, get your fingers into the wool, and heave. It comes away in a greasy rope, and the striker swings free. The bell answers at once, one huge flat note that shudders through the frame and rolls out over the reef.
      *goto bell_b2_rung
    *else
      You get as high as the crossbeam before your hands slide on the lard. The wool is packed tighter than it looked, and you spend the moments you had hauling at it with fingers that will not close.
      *goto bell_b2_wrecked
  # Throw stones at the bronze until the harbor hears it.${hint_bell_dex}
    *set check_stat "dex"
    *set check_dc 12
    *set check_skill "Ring the Bell"
    *if (weapon_type = "ranged")
      *set advantage true
    *gosub_scene dice_engine roll_d20_check
    *if (check_success)
      *if (weapon_type = "ranged")
        You skip the hunt for a stone and send your first shot from the ${weapon} into the bronze. It rings. You send the next one after it.
      *else
        The third stone hits the bronze dead center. The note goes out over the reef, raw and ugly and impossible to miss, and you keep throwing until your arm burns.
      *goto bell_b2_rung
    *else
      The first stone skips off the granite. The second goes into the sea. The third clips the frame, and the wood only thuds. By the time you have a fourth in your hand, the mast lanterns are level with the foam.
      *goto bell_b2_wrecked
  # Follow the cord to the cleat and slip the hitch.${hint_bell_int}
    *set check_stat "int"
    *set check_dc 12
    *set check_skill "Slip the Hitch"
    *gosub_scene dice_engine roll_d20_check
    *if (check_success)
      You follow the cord down the post with your fingers to the cleat, where somebody has taken three neat turns and a hitch. It is tied to be untied by someone who knows the knot, and you do. A pull, a shake, and the striker throws the wad off on its next swing. The bell answers before the wool reaches the water.
      *goto bell_b2_rung
    *else
      The cord is wet, the hitch has swollen, and in the dark you cannot find the end. You pull the wrong turn and lock it tighter, and by the time you find the right one, the lanterns are much closer.
      *goto bell_b2_wrecked
  *if (known_mage_hand)
    # [Cantrip: Mage Hand] Send a pale hand up the frame to pull the wool free.
      A hand of pale force rises along the post, closes on the wad of wool, and pulls. The wool comes away with a wet tearing sound, and the striker swings free. The bell rings.
      *goto bell_b2_rung
  *if (known_minor_illusion)
    # [Cantrip: Minor Illusion] Throw the toll of a bell out over the reef.
      You shape the sound in your mind, then let it go. A deep, slow stroke rolls out of the empty air above the reef, and then another, exactly where the real bell should be.
      *goto bell_b2_rung

*label bell_b2_rung
Out on the water, the two mast lanterns swing hard to the left. A thin voice carries across the swell, someone on that deck shouting for a lead-line. The lights slide on past the foam and go steadily toward the harbor mouth until the haze takes them.

*page_break The oars stop below the ladder…
*goto bell_b3

*label bell_b2_wrecked
*set bell_quest_stage "failed"
*set bell_resolution "wrecked"

The lanterns do not turn. A long, tearing crack rolls across the water, and one of the mast lights swings, tilts and goes out. Voices carry over the swell, a lot of them, high and quick, and then fewer. Below the ladder a pair of oars begins to dip, and the rowboat you saw slides away into the murk toward the sound.

Nobody comes along the causeway. You stay at the frame post because there is nothing else to do with your hands, and you keep at the wool until it tears loose and the striker swings free. The bell rolls out one huge slow stroke over a reef that already has its ship.

*page_break Back toward the docks…
*goto port_valen_harbor_pois

*label bell_b3
*temp bell_fought false
*set hours_to_pass 0
*set minutes_to_pass 15
*set time_advance_call_id "bell_b3_time"
*gosub_scene calendar advance_time
*gosub_scene calendar recalc_hp_from_neglect

*if (hp_current <= 0)
  *set death_cause "starvation"
  *if (neglect_damage_fatigue > neglect_damage_hunger)
    *set death_cause "exhaustion"
  *goto_scene death death_screen

Below the head of the stone, the oars stop. The boat bumps the foot of the ladder, and two men are coming up the steps fast. One has the rag-stuffed lantern swinging from his wrist. The other is broad in the shoulder, with a hatchet hanging from his fist, its head pitted with rust. They stop three steps down, breathing hard. The broad one looks at the bell, then at you, then along the causeway toward the harbor, and his eyes stay there a moment too long.

"That wasn't yours to ring," he says.

*label bell_b3_hub
*temp hint_bell_cha ""
*temp hint_bell_str2 ""
*temp hint_bell_wis ""
*if (show_stat_hints)
  *if (guidance_active)
    *set hint_bell_cha " [CHA DC 12 (+1d4 Guidance)]"
    *set hint_bell_str2 " [STR DC 12 (+1d4 Guidance)]"
    *set hint_bell_wis " [WIS DC 12 (+1d4 Guidance)]"
  *else
    *set hint_bell_cha " [CHA DC 12]"
    *set hint_bell_str2 " [STR DC 12]"
    *set hint_bell_wis " [WIS DC 12]"

*choice
  *if ((known_guidance) and (not(guidance_active)))
    # [Cantrip: Guidance] Murmur the focusing verse and let your breathing slow.
      *set guidance_active true
      You breathe the verse out, and the pounding in your ears drops to something you can think through.
      *goto bell_b3_hub
  # Tell them the whole harbor heard that bell, and the Watch is coming.${hint_bell_cha}
    *gosub_scene dice_engine roll_skill_check 12 "persuasion" "" "" "" "" ""
    *if (check_success)
      "Every watchman between here and the customs tower heard that, and half the harbor with them," you say. "You've got as long as it takes to walk here from the stairs. Whatever is in that boat is worth less than what they'll do to you." The lantern man is already looking down the causeway. Out past the stairs, a light has started to bob.
      *goto bell_b3_bribe
    *else
      "Nobody's coming," says the broad man. He has been listening to the same night you have, and there are no whistles in it yet. He comes up the last three steps with the hatchet swinging loose at his knee.
      *set combat_enemy_advantage true
      *goto bell_fight
  # Plant yourself at the head of the steps and do not move.${hint_bell_str2}
    *gosub_scene dice_engine roll_skill_check 12 "athletics" "" "" "" "" ""
    *if (check_success)
      You plant your feet on the wet stone at the head of the steps, square across the only way up, and stay there. The lantern man stops. The broad one looks at the width of the steps, at the drop on either side, and at you, and he does not come up. Neither of you moves for the length of a long breath, and the sea works at the stone below.
      *goto bell_b3_bribe
    *else
      The step under your left foot is green with weed, and it goes when you shift your weight. You catch the frame post with one hand and lose your footing on the other, and the broad man takes the step you gave him.
      *set disadvantage true
      *goto bell_fight
  # Watch the broad man's eyes and give him a way out.${hint_bell_wis}
    *gosub_scene dice_engine roll_skill_check 12 "insight" "" "" "" "" ""
    *if (check_success)
      You watch his eyes and not the hatchet. They go to the causeway again and again, and each time they come back a little slower. "You didn't see a face," you say. "All you saw was a bell that wanted ringing. Take your boat." It is the offer he was waiting for.
      *goto bell_b3_bribe
    *else
      "You can take your boat and go," you say, and your voice comes out thin. The broad man hears how thin. The hatchet comes up.
      *set combat_enemy_advantage true
      *set disadvantage true
      *goto bell_fight

*label bell_b3_bribe
The broad man swears through his teeth and drags a leather pouch off his belt. He throws it, and it lands on the step at your feet with a heavy clink. "For your trouble, bellringer," he says. "You never saw a face." Then he is going down the ladder, and the lantern man goes after him, and the oars bite. The boat slides off into the murk.

The whistle comes down the causeway then, two long notes, and a lantern out past the stairs breaks into a run.

*page_break The Watch reaches the stone…
*goto bell_b4

*label bell_won

The broad man goes down on the step and does not get up. The lantern man is sprawled at the foot of the ladder with his sling under him, and the boat is drifting away on its painter. A leather pouch has burst partway open on the stone where the broad man fell, and silver shows through the split. Along the causeway, a whistle sounds, two long notes.

*page_break The Watch reaches the stone…
*goto bell_b4

*label bell_rescued
Your knees go. The hatchet lifts, and a whistle shrieks along the causeway, two long notes, and it does not fall. The boat is already backing off the ladder when the lantern light reaches the head of the stone, and two watchmen find you sitting against the frame post with a hand pressed to your side. "Get down to the quay," the older one says, not unkindly. "We have this."

  *set bell_quest_stage "resolved"

*page_break Back along the causeway…

*label bell_fled
You run. The causeway is a long dark road, and behind you the hatchet rings off the frame post where you were standing. Halfway to the stairs you meet two lanterns coming the other way at a run, and they part around you without slowing. The older watchman calls out as he passes. "Get on home. We have this."

Two watchmen in greased sheepskins reach the head of the breakwater, a lantern swinging between them. The older man stops with both hands on his knees to get his breath back.

*if (not(pv_slip_seen))
  *set pv_slip_seen true
  The Fishmongers' Slip is a broad ramp of wet granite that runs down into the harbor beside the fish market at the western end of the waterfront, sloped so the inshore boats can be hauled up on rollers and sold off the boards. Scale glitter lies in every crack of the stone. Behind the stalls stand the low black smokehouses, their roofs stained brown by years of smoke, and above them a long net-drying loft with its loading door swinging open to the wind.
*else
  The Fishmongers' Slip again, with brine on the stone and smoke on the wind.

*goto pv_poi_fish_slip_menu

*label pv_poi_fish_slip_menu
*comment Re-entry line: a pool of three pure-prose variants per open state (see harbor_pick in
*comment port_valen_harbor_pois), then the weather line.
*temp fish_pick 0
*rand fish_pick 1 3
*if (((time_period = "Dusk") or (time_period = "Night")) or (weather_severity >= 3))
  *if (weather_severity >= 3)
    Every boat is hauled high and lashed down, and the stalls are boarded over. Spray drives up the ramp in sheets, and a lone fishwife is dragging the last tub of salt into a smokehouse with her shoulder against the door. Nothing is being bought or sold in this.
  *elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
    The stalls are packing down for the night. Fishwives wash the boards with harbor water while gulls fight over the scraps, and the smokehouse doors are being barred one by one. The last hand-carts rattle up the ramp with the day's salt barrels.
  *else
    The slip is dark and empty, the stalls shuttered and the smokehouses barred. The harbor slaps at the foot of the ramp, and high above it the net-loft door creaks on its hinge. Nothing here will move again before the flood brings the first boats.
  *page_break Back up to the quayside…
  *goto port_valen_harbor_pois

*if (time_period = "Pre-Dawn")
  *if (fish_pick = 1)
    Lanterns bob across the harbor as the inshore boats come in on the flood, and crews are already rolling the first of them up the ramp. A slate stands ready on the auction block, and the fishwives are blowing on their hands beside their empty tubs.
  *elseif (fish_pick = 2)
    The slip wakes in the dark. Lanterns swing at the mouths of the smokehouses, the boats grind against the foot of the ramp with the flood, and crews haul the first baskets up in silence while the auctioneer chalks his slate.
  *else
    Boat lanterns crowd the harbor mouth, riding in on the flood, and the ramp is already loud with keels and boots. Fishwives lay out their knives and slate boards in the lamplight, and the block waits for the first lot.
*elseif (time_period = "Morning")
  *if (fish_pick = 1)
    The block is in full voice, a hand-bell ringing between lots while crews tip their baskets out along the wet stone. Buyers push along the rail shouting bids, and fishwives call prices over their shoulders while their knives never stop.
  *elseif (fish_pick = 2)
    The auction is at its height. A hand-bell rings between lots, baskets of silver fish slide across the wet stone, and the buyers at the rail bellow their bids over one another while the fishwives gut and sluice without looking up.
  *else
    Morning on the slip is one long shout. The hand-bell clangs, baskets go over on the stone, gulls dive at the offcuts, and every fishwife on the ramp has a price and a temper ready for anyone who slows the line.
*elseif (time_period = "Midday")
  *if (fish_pick = 1)
    The block is quiet now. Fishwives work down the stalls with knives and wet straw, and the smokehouse doors stand open on drifting blue smoke. A few late buyers pick over the leftovers while the gulls wait their turn on the rail.
  *elseif (fish_pick = 2)
    The rush is over and the slip is slowing to a chew. Crews rinse the block with harbor water, knives scrape on boards, and the smokehouse doors stand open on lazy blue smoke that smells of oak and salt.
  *else
    Past the noon bell the auction has thinned to haggling over the last tubs. Fishwives sit on upturned baskets to split the afternoon's salting, and the smokehouses breathe blue smoke across the ramp while the harbor slaps at the foot of the stone.
*else
  *if (fish_pick = 1)
    The last of the day's catch is being split for salting, and the smokehouses breathe a warm, sweet haze across the ramp. Fishwives work down the tubs in pairs, and the gulls have stopped bothering to fight over what is left.
  *elseif (fish_pick = 2)
    Salt goes down in fistfuls as the day's catch is split and layered into barrels, and the smokehouses send a sweet blue haze drifting over the ramp. The fishwives talk low and fast, and the gulls sit fat on the rail.
  *else
    The slip has settled into its afternoon work of splitting, salting and stacking. Barrels rumble up the ramp on hand-carts, smoke leaks from the smokehouse eaves, and the auction block stands scrubbed and empty beneath the net-loft.
*if (weather = "Rain")
  Rain runs down the granite in a sheet, carrying scales toward the harbor.
*elseif (weather = "Fog")
  Fog has swallowed the harbor mouth, and boats appear at the foot of the ramp one lantern at a time.
*elseif ((weather = "Snow") or (weather = "Sleet"))
  Sleet ticks off the stall awnings, and the fishwives stamp their boots on trampled straw.
*elseif (weather = "Clear")
  Sun flashes off the scales on the stones, and gulls wheel low over the stalls.

*label pv_poi_fish_stalls
Stalls line both sides of the ramp, each with a fishwife behind a plank of wet slate, a knife, and a bucket. Steam and the smell of hot oil hang under the awnings.

*label pv_poi_fish_gossip
You lean against a smokehouse door frame with your back to the warm planks and let the fishwives talk over their knives.
*if (fish_quest_stage = "unstarted")
  The talk runs to the price of salt, an overdue boat from the northern banks, and one grievance that comes up in three different mouths: bass fetched twice as much at the block last winter. When they get to the reason, all of them turn their heads toward the man in the good gloves at the head of the ramp, and then back to their knives.
*elseif (fish_quest_stage = "active")
  The fishwives have quieted around you. Somebody has seen you talking to the widow, and they are watching to see what you do about it.
*elseif (fish_resolution = "lawful")
  The fishwives are loud again. Three buyers have been struck off the block for the season, and the market inspector is being bought onions by people who never used to speak to him.
*elseif (fish_resolution = "pragmatic")
  The fishwives keep their voices low while you are near. The half circle at the block still bids by the brim of a cap, and a new face stands in it now.
*elseif (fish_resolution = "strategic")
  The talk is about the morning a stranger put silver in the dish and bid against the smokehouse men. The buyers bid against each other now, and the widow's boy has been seen eating meat.
*else
  *comment fish_quest_stage = "failed"
  The talk stops when you settle against the door. It starts again a few doors down.

You take a place at the rail along the top of the block. Below it, boats are hauled up the rollers one at a time and their baskets tipped out in a row on the wet stone, and a hoarse auctioneer with a hand-bell and a slate chalks each lot as the crews empty it. A dozen buyers stand in a half circle: smokehouse men in leather aprons and cook-house runners with baskets on their hips.

*if (fish_quest_stage = "resolved")
  *if (fish_resolution = "lawful")
    The market inspector stands beside the auctioneer with his slate under his arm, and three buyers' places in the half circle are empty. The bidding is loud and quick, and a cook-house runner wins a lot of bass with a whoop that sets the gulls off.
  *elseif (fish_resolution = "pragmatic")

*if (not(fish_met_wenna))
  The first lot is herring, and it goes the way you would expect. Three buyers bid against one another until the price has climbed a copper past where it started, and the bell falls on a man who wanted them badly.

  The second lot is different. A woman in a salt-stiff smock stands beside three baskets of fat sea bass, silver-bellied and still moving, and the auctioneer calls a price so low that a runner beside you laughs out loud. Nobody bids. A man in good leather gloves, in the middle of the half circle, lifts two fingers to the brim of his cap. The buyer on his left scratches an ear. The buyer on his right looks at his boots. The bell falls on the man in the good gloves, and the price is half of what the herring fetched by the basket.

  The woman counts the coins into a leather purse, twice, and carries the empty baskets down the ramp with her lips pressed white.

*else

*label fish_wenna_talk
*set fish_met_wenna true
*if (fish_quest_stage = "unstarted")
  *set fish_quest_stage "active"

You catch up with her at the foot of the ramp, where a flat-bottomed skiff sits on its rollers and a boy of about fifteen is scraping scales off the thwarts with the back of a knife. The woman is knotting a cord around her empty baskets with hands that do not want to be gentle.

"You were at the rail," she says, without turning. "You saw."

"I saw nobody bid."

"Nobody ever bids on mine." She pulls the knot tight. "Wenna Rusk. The [i]Patience[/i] is mine, and she was my husband's until he went over the side off the northern banks last spring. Bass goes for twenty coppers a basket on a fair morning. Ten is what I got." She lifts the baskets onto the barrow. "Three dawns running. The boy and I split what is left after the rent on the rollers, and some mornings it is not enough to buy the salt for tomorrow's catch."

"Why sell here?"

"Inshore catch goes through the block. That is the warden's rule, and it is a good one, because it keeps the boats honest." Her mouth twists. "It doesn't keep the buyers honest. That is Thale, in the good gloves. He owns two of the smokehouses, and the other two buyers eat at his table. The warden says a quiet market is no crime, and he is right. There is no law against nobody wanting my fish."

She has two baskets of brill left, held back for the last lot of the morning. She looks at them, and then at you, the way people look at a stranger who has asked one question too many.

"I am not asking you for anything," she says. "But you asked."

*goto fish_block_hub

*label fish_climax_hub
The auctioneer lifts his hand-bell. The last lot of the morning is Wenna's two baskets of brill, and the half circle is drifting a step closer together.

*choice
  # Walk to the market inspector's booth and lay out what you have seen.
    *goto fish_route_lawful
  # Step into the half circle and lift two fingers to your own brow.

  *selectable_if ((((gold * 100) + (silver * 10)) + copper) >= 40) # Put four silver marks in the auctioneer's dish and bid against the ring. [4 Silver Marks]
    *goto fish_route_strategic
  # Step back from the rail and think it over.
    *goto fish_block_hub

The market inspector keeps a plank booth at the head of the ramp, with a lantern on a hook, a slate of licence marks, and a fish-scale weight hung on a chain to prove every basket. He is a heavy man in a Watch-blue surcoat, and he is eating an onion the way another man would eat an apple. He looks at you, then at the block, and goes on chewing.

The warden stops chewing. He comes out of the booth and stands beside you at the rail, and when the last lot comes up he watches the half circle and not the fish. Two fingers to a brim. An ear scratched. A glance at a boot.

"Huh," he says. He takes the chalk from behind his ear and writes three marks on his slate, and when the bell lifts he walks into the half circle and takes the auctioneer by the sleeve. The lot is called again, this time with three buyers' places empty. A cook-house runner bids, then another, and the bell falls on a price that makes Wenna put a hand to her mouth.

The warden fishes a small purse off his belt and counts three silver marks into your palm. "Informant's share of the fine," he says. "It's in the rules. Nobody ever claims it."

He slides your stake across the dish into Wenna's purse in one movement. A cook-house runner has been watching the whole thing with his mouth open, and he buys the baskets off you for what you paid before you have decided what to do with them.

Nobody in the half circle looks at you now. The buyers who used to stand a yard apart are standing a yard apart for a different reason. On the next lot, the man to Thale's left bids against him.

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*comment THE DRILL SQUARE (pv_poi_carrion): the compound's training place for level 2
*comment Free, 4 hours a session, once a day, and it cannot fail. It only ever gets a recruit to level 2; later levels have other
*comment teachers. The counts live in character_progression.txt (training_preview, do_training_session, refresh_level_up_progress); this file only
*comment prints the drill. The session's prose is picked from training_sessions (which do_training_session has already counted on
*comment this page), never from the clock or *rand, so a replayed page prints the same words. Vanguard (Sergeant Varren) is written;
*comment Scouts (Kestrel) and Cadre (Ysolde) use the file-corporal set until their own is written. To add a squad, add its six
*comment vignettes and its talk lines here; nothing that trains ever needs to change.
*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

*label pv_poi_carrion
*looplimit 100000
*set current_district "compound"
*set pv_drill_seen true
*gosub_scene calendar update_hub_condition
*gosub_scene character_progression training_preview
*temp dr_day false
*if ((time_period = "Morning") or (time_period = "Midday"))
  *set dr_day true
*temp dr_done false
*if (training_sessions >= level_up_sessions_needed)
