[b]◈ THE DRILL SQUARE[/b]
*line_break
[b][⏳ Port Valen Hour: ${clock_time} (${time_period}) | Hit Points: ${hp_current}/${hp_max}${hub_condition_str}][/b]

*comment ── LAYER 1: PLACE ──
A rail fence rings a wide square of packed ground behind the barracks, scuffed bare by years of boots. Practice posts stand in rows down the middle, their tops battered pale. A lean-to at the near end holds racks of wooden practice weapons, a stack of round wooden shields, and a water barrel gone green at the rim. The watchtower looks down on one corner and the seawall closes off the far side.

*comment ── LAYER 2: SKY & LIGHT (no people) ──
*if (weather = "Rain")
  Rain drums on the lean-to roof and pocks the earth, and the whole yard has turned to dark slop that sucks at every step.
*elseif (weather = "Storm")
  Wind drives the rain across the yard in sheets, and the racks rattle against their posts.
*elseif (weather = "Fog")
  Sea-fog lies low over the yard, blurring the far posts to grey shapes and beading on every rail.
*elseif (weather = "Snow")
  Wet snow settles on the rails and the lean-to roof, and the earth under it has gone stiff and slick.
*elseif (weather = "Sleet")
  Sleet ticks on the lean-to roof and freezes in the ruts.
*elseif (weather = "Blizzard")
  Snow drives flat across the open earth until the far fence is gone.
*elseif (weather = "Clear")
  *if (time_period = "Night")
    A cold moon stands over the seawall and lays the shadows of the posts across the earth.
  *elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
    The low sun turns the rail fence amber and throws the shadow of every post halfway across the yard.
  *elseif (time_period = "Pre-Dawn")
    The sky over the seawall is grey and cold, and the first light has not reached the earth.
  *else
    Clear daylight fills the yard and shows every scuff and boot-print in the earth.
*else
  *comment Overcast
  *if (time_period = "Night")
    Low cloud hides the stars, and the yard is dark beyond the reach of the gate braziers.
  *elseif (time_period = "Pre-Dawn")
    Low cloud keeps the sky black, and only the gate braziers light the edge of the yard.
  *else
    Low grey cloud sits over the yard, and the seawall beyond it fades into the same colour.

*if ((ground_state = "sodden") and (not(weather_wet)))
  Days of rain have left the yard a churned bog, with standing water filling every rut.
*elseif ((ground_state = "wet") and (not(weather_wet)))
  The ground is still soft from the last rain, and it holds every boot-print.

*comment ── LAYER 3: WHO IS ABOUT ──
*if ((weather_severity >= 3) and ((dr_day) or (time_period = "Dusk")))
  Drill has been called off. The racks are chained shut, the posts stand alone in the wind, and every squad has gone under cover in the barracks.
*elseif (dr_day)
  *if (squad = "vanguard")
    Sergeant Varren has a file of shield-bearers drawn up along the far rail. He walks behind them with one heavy hand out, thumping the rim of any shield that sags, and the whole line shuffles a half-step in answer.
  *elseif (squad = "scouts")
    Kestrel crouches at the near rail with two recruits, tracing a line in the dirt with one finger. Past them, a line of Scouts nocks and looses at the posts in a steady, unhurried rhythm, and nobody is shouting anything at all.
  *elseif (squad = "cadre")
    Ysolde stands apart from the rest with a slate in one hand, watching a line of Cadre recruits hold a candle flame steady between two fingers. She marks the slate without looking up whenever one of them flinches.
  *else
    File corporals have the recruits strung out in ragged lines across the square, calling the count for stance drills and cuffing anyone who drifts off the mark.
*elseif ((time_period = "Dusk") or (time_period = "Afternoon"))
  The day's drill is done. The last ranks file off toward the wash-troughs and the cookhouse smoke while a corporal chains the racks shut.
*else
  The yard lies empty. A brazier burns at the gate, and the posts and racks stand in the dark until the morning drill.

*choice
  *if (dr_open)
    # Drill with your squad. [~4 Hours]
      *goto pv_carrion_drill
  *if (dr_done)
    # Drill with your squad: nothing more the yard can teach you.
      *goto pv_carrion_drill_done
  *if ((not(dr_done)) and (not(training_can_train)))
    # Drill with your squad: you have done your shift for the day.
      *goto pv_carrion_drill_closed
  *if (((not(dr_done)) and (training_can_train)) and (not(dr_day)))
    # Drill with your squad: the yard is shut until morning.
      *goto pv_carrion_drill_closed
  *if ((((not(dr_done)) and (training_can_train)) and (dr_day)) and (weather_severity >= 3))
    # Drill with your squad: called off for the weather.
      *goto pv_carrion_drill_closed
  *if (leaders_at_drill)
    # Ask your squad leader how you are coming along.
      *goto pv_carrion_talk
  *if (not(leaders_at_drill))
    # Ask your squad leader how you are coming along: they are not on the yard now.
      *goto pv_carrion_leader_away
  # Head back across the compound.
    *goto port_valen_hub

*comment Closure page for the drill button above. It costs no time and sets nothing. It reads the clock and the derived flags directly
*comment (not the temps the yard set), so it plays from any label.
*label pv_carrion_drill_closed
*gosub_scene calendar update_hub_condition
*gosub_scene character_progression training_preview
[b]◈ THE DRILL SQUARE[/b]
*line_break
*temp dr_day false
*if ((time_period = "Morning") or (time_period = "Midday"))
  *set dr_day true
*if (not(training_can_train))
  You have already put in a full shift on the yard today. The corporals are not handing out a second one, and a tired recruit is a hazard to everyone in the line.
*elseif ((time_period = "Dusk") or (time_period = "Afternoon"))
  Drill is over for the day. The racks are chained shut and the last of the ranks are filing off toward the cookhouse.
*elseif (not(dr_day))
  The yard lies dark and the racks are chained shut. Drill begins with the light.
*else
  The yard is empty. Drill has been called off, the racks are chained shut, and nobody is going out onto the open earth until the weather eases.
*page_break Back across the compound…
*goto pv_poi_carrion

*label pv_carrion_drill_done
[b]◈ THE DRILL SQUARE[/b]
*line_break
*if ((squad = "vanguard") and (leaders_at_drill))
  Sergeant Varren stops you at the rail with one hand flat against your chest. He looks you up and down, the milk-pale eye a beat behind the good one. "You've had the yard," he says. "Whatever I can put into you on it, you've got. What you do with it is your business."
*elseif ((squad = "scouts") and (leaders_at_drill))
  Kestrel doesn't look up from the line. "You've had the ground," she says. "What you do with it now is yours to carry."
*elseif ((squad = "cadre") and (leaders_at_drill))
  Ysolde marks her slate without looking up. "You've had the yard," she says. "What you carry out of it now is your own risk."
*else
  The corporal at the rail waves you off the ground before you have crossed it. "You've had all the yard has," he says. "Nothing left here I can put you through."
*page_break Back across the compound…
*goto pv_poi_carrion

*comment The squad leaders are on the yard in the morning only (leaders_at_drill). Outside that this page costs no time and sets nothing, and tells the player where to find them.
*label pv_carrion_leader_away
*gosub_scene calendar update_hub_condition
[b]◈ THE DRILL SQUARE[/b]
*line_break
*if (squad = "vanguard")
  The rail where Varren stands is empty. He runs the Vanguard on the square in the morning, and once the drill is done he goes back to the armory shed.
*elseif (squad = "scouts")
  The near rail is empty. Kestrel runs the Scouts on the ground in the morning, and once the drill is done she is gone, and nobody on the square will say where.
*elseif (squad = "cadre")
  The slate is gone from the rail. Ysolde runs the Cadre on the square in the morning, and once the drill is done she goes back to her workroom.
*else
  The corporal's pine tally-board is gone from the rail. He is on the yard in the morning and nowhere you would think to look after that.
*page_break Back across the square…
*goto pv_poi_carrion

*comment Talking to the squad leader: the words say how the training and the finished work are going, and the numbers stay in the bracket.
*label pv_carrion_talk
*gosub_scene character_progression refresh_level_up_progress
[b]◈ THE DRILL SQUARE[/b]
*line_break
*if (squad = "vanguard")
  Sergeant Varren stands at the rail with his arms folded over his chest, watching the ranks turn. He does not look round when you stop beside him.

  *if (training_sessions = 0)
    "You haven't been on my ground yet." He keeps his eyes on the ranks. "It's open in daylight. Come and stand in a file."
  *elseif (training_sessions < 10)
    "Your feet are learning," he grunts. "Your head isn't there yet. Keep coming."
  *elseif (training_sessions < 20)
    "Better." A short nod. "You hold a stance now without having to think about holding it."
  *elseif (training_sessions < level_up_sessions_needed)
    "Nearly. A file wants people who don't need telling twice, and you are getting to be one of them."
  *else
    "That's the yard finished with you."

  *if (quests_finished < (level_up_quests_needed / 2))
    "Drill's only half of it. The rest is work done off the ground and finished properly, and you've barely started on it."
  *elseif (quests_finished < level_up_quests_needed)
    "The other half is work done off the ground and finished properly. You've put in a fair share. Put in more."
  *else
    "And you've finished the work off the ground as well. There's nothing I'd hold you back for."

  *if (level_up_ready)
    "Then you're ready," he says at last, and turns back to the ranks. "Whenever you want the next step, it's yours to take."
*elseif (squad = "scouts")
  Kestrel crouches at the rail, watching the line without seeming to watch it, one arrow loose between two fingers. She does not look round when you stop beside her.

  *if (training_sessions = 0)
    "Haven't seen you out here." Her eyes stay on the posts. "Ground's open by day. Come and stand in it."
  *elseif (training_sessions < 10)
    "You still think with your feet," she says. "Most people do, at your stage."
  *elseif (training_sessions < 20)
    "Quieter," she says. "You're starting to move like the ground matters."
  *elseif (training_sessions < level_up_sessions_needed)
    "Close. A scout who has to think about being quiet has already failed at it. You're nearly past that."
  *else
    "That's what I have to teach."

  *if (quests_finished < (level_up_quests_needed / 2))
    "The ground's only half the job. The rest is work finished out past the gate, and you haven't brought back much of that yet."
  *elseif (quests_finished < level_up_quests_needed)
    "The rest is work finished out past the gate. You've brought back a fair share. Bring back more."
  *else
    "And you've brought back the rest of it too. There's nothing left here that a job wouldn't teach faster."

  *if (level_up_ready)
    "Then you're past what I can put in front of you," she says, and turns back to the line. "Whenever you want it, the next step is yours."
*elseif (squad = "cadre")
  Ysolde looks up from her slate before you have finished crossing the yard, mercury-silver eyes unhurried and entirely unsurprised to see you.

  *if (training_sessions = 0)
    "You haven't been out here." She marks the slate. "The ground's open by day. Come and be counted."
  *elseif (training_sessions < 10)
    "You're still flinching," she says. "Expected, at this stage. Keep coming."
  *elseif (training_sessions < 20)
    "Better," she says. "Your hand held steady twice today. I wrote down both."
  *elseif (training_sessions < level_up_sessions_needed)
    "Nearly there. I'd trust you with a spell now, though I wouldn't stand quite so close while you held it."
  *else
    "The yard's got nothing left to teach you."

  *if (quests_finished < (level_up_quests_needed / 2))
    "The yard's half of it. The rest is work done properly, out where I can't mark it, and you haven't done much of that."
  *elseif (quests_finished < level_up_quests_needed)
    "The rest is work done properly, out where I can't mark it. You've done a fair share. Do more."
  *else
    "And you've done that as well. I've got nothing left to hold back."

  *if (level_up_ready)
    "Then you're ready," she says, and returns to the slate. "Whenever you want the next step, take it."
*else
  The corporal at the rail is a lean man with a notched pine tally-board under one arm and a voice worn down to gravel. He looks you over before he answers.

  *if (training_sessions = 0)
    "Never seen you on my ground." He looks past you at the lines. "It's open in daylight. Come and stand in a line like everyone else."
  *elseif (training_sessions < 10)
    "You're learning to stand," he grunts. "Standing's most of it. Keep coming."
  *elseif (training_sessions < 20)
    "You've stopped falling over," he mutters. "That's more than I can say for some."
  *elseif (training_sessions < level_up_sessions_needed)
    "Nearly there. I'd put you in a line now without checking your feet first."
  *else
    "There's nothing more I can put you through," he admits. "You've had what the yard has."

  *if (quests_finished < (level_up_quests_needed / 2))
    "Drill's half of it, though. The rest is work done off the ground and finished properly, and you've barely started on it."
  *elseif (quests_finished < level_up_quests_needed)
    "The rest is work done off the ground and finished properly. You've put in a fair share. Put in more."
  *else
    "And you've finished the work off the ground as well. I've nothing to hold you back for."

  *if (level_up_ready)
    "So that's you ready," he says, and jerks his chin toward the barracks. "Whenever you want the next step, it's yours."
*line_break
[b][🎯 Training ${training_sessions} / ${level_up_sessions_needed} | Quests ${quests_finished} / ${level_up_quests_needed}][/b]
*page_break Back across the square…
*goto pv_poi_carrion

*comment The session itself: 4 hours (training_hours) through the shared time engine, with outdoor exposure, then the shared counter.
*comment do_training_session runs BEFORE any prose, so training_sessions is already this session's number: it picks the vignette
*comment (a rotation of six, so no two sessions in a row match) and marks the first and last session. A replay finds the same number.
*label pv_carrion_drill
*set hours_to_pass training_hours
*set minutes_to_pass 0
*set exposure_base_mins (training_hours * 60)
*gosub_scene calendar calc_exposure
*set time_advance_call_id "pv_carrion_drill_1"
*gosub_scene calendar advance_time
*gosub_scene calendar recalc_hp_from_neglect
*if (hp_current <= 0)
  *set death_cause "starvation"
  *if (neglect_damage_fatigue > neglect_damage_hunger)
    *set death_cause "exhaustion"
  *goto_scene death death_screen
*gosub_scene character_progression do_training_session
*temp dr_start (((clock_hour + 24) - training_hours) modulo 24)
*temp dr_pick (training_sessions modulo 6)
[b]◈ THE DRILL SQUARE[/b]
*line_break
*comment Opening line: the hour the session started (derived from the clock at its end) and whether it is wet.
*if (dr_start <= 11)
  *if (weather_wet)
    Drill begins in a thin rain. The ground is already slick, and every step out onto it takes more thought than it should.
  *else
    You go out into the cold early light, your breath showing and the ground hard underfoot.
*elseif (dr_start <= 13)
  *if (weather_wet)
    By the middle of the day you are wet through before the first order is out.
  *else
    The middle of the day finds the yard at its busiest, with the racks already half empty by the time you reach them.
*else
  *if (weather_wet)
    The afternoon drips steadily from the rail and the lean-to eaves as you take your place.
  *else
    The noise from the rest of the compound thins behind you as other details finish, and you take your place in the afternoon light.

*if (squad = "vanguard")
  *if (training_sessions = 1)
    Varren looks you over once, from your feet to your shoulders, and points to a place in the file. "Second rank," he says.

  *if (dr_pick = 0)
    Varren puts you in the second rank of a shield file and hands you a round wooden shield from the rack. On his word the front rank leans back into you, and you take their weight through your legs while a corporal walks behind with a pole, prodding any heel that slides. "Hold," Varren says, and then nothing more, until your thighs shake and your fingers have gone numb on the strap.
    *if (character_class = "barbarian")
      Every part of you wants to shove back and be done with it. You keep your feet planted and your weight low, and let the push burn out through your legs.
    *else
      You feel where the line is weakest before he says it, and lean your weight over to cover the gap.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 1)
    Two files lock shields against a weighted sledge and push it down the length of the square while Varren paces beside it. Sandbags shift under the runners, and the sledge slews sideways every time one rank pushes out of step. He does not shout. He stamps down on the heel of whoever is late, and the sledge straightens.
    *if (character_class = "barbarian")
      You push out of step twice, hard enough to slew the sledge, and his boot lands beside your heel both times until you find the beat.
    *else
      You settle into the count and stay on it, and after the second length the sledge slides as if it weighed nothing.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 2)
    Varren drags a heel through the ground to draw a straight line, then a second one a shield's length behind it. Your rank steps to the first, stops, steps back to the second, stops, over and over, until the ground between the lines is churned to powder. Halfway through he walks the length of the file and stands a pace behind you, watching your feet and not your face.
    *if (character_class = "barbarian")
      Stopping dead on a line is harder than charging past it. You learn how much harder around the twentieth time.
    *else
      After a while the lines stop being lines. Your feet find them without your eyes.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 3)
    The files break into pairs with wooden weapons from the rack, and Varren works his way along them, stopping each bout with a raised palm. He shows one corporal where an elbow drifted and a recruit where a shoulder dropped. When he reaches you the bout runs on for a long count before the palm comes up. "Again," he says.
    *if (character_class = "barbarian")
      Your partner backs off two paces and you go after them harder than the bout wants. The palm comes up sharp, and you draw back with your breath sawing.
    *else
      Your bout runs long, clean and dull, and the palm comes up at last with a grunt that might be approval.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 4)
    Varren sets a hard loop around the fence and hangs a sand-filled pack on every back. Once round, then twice, until the loop has stopped being a distance and become a length of time. Anyone who drops a pack picks it up and carries it a lap longer. Nobody argues.
    *if (character_class = "barbarian")
      Heat builds behind your ribs by the third lap, and you swallow it, one step and then the next.
    *else
      You settle the pack high, breathe on the steps, and let the laps count themselves.
  *else
    A corporal at the far rail hurls sacks of wet sand over the fence, and they slam into the shield rims like thrown stones. Varren calls the turns, and the rank swings its shields left, then right, then drops behind them together. Anyone who flinches takes the next sack in the ribs.
    *if (character_class = "barbarian")
      You take the first sack in the ribs and grunt and stay upright, and the second lands square on the rim.
    *else
      You keep the rim up and the knees bent, and the sacks stop landing on you and start landing on the shield.

  *if ((training_sessions = level_up_sessions_needed) and (level_up_sessions_needed > 0))
    When the file is dismissed, Varren stops you at the rail with a hand flat against your chest. He looks at you for a long moment, then nods once and turns back to the yard.
*elseif (squad = "scouts")
  *if (training_sessions = 1)
    Kestrel looks at you once, head to boots, and jerks her chin at a gap at the end of the group.

  *if (dr_pick = 0)
    Kestrel has marked a run of flat stones across open ground and set two Scouts at the far end to listen for you. You cross them one at a time, weight settling before the next step, while a boot scrape somewhere behind you earns a flat, unimpressed look from the far end of the line.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 1)
    A row of straw-wrapped posts wait at range, and Kestrel calls a count under her breath while the row of Scouts looses in turn. Her only reaction to a center hit or a wide miss is the same small tick of her chalk against her tally.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 2)
    Kestrel walks a stretch of turned earth and stops, tapping a boot-print with the toe of her own boot. She waits while you read it back to her: which foot, how heavy, how long since it was made. Get it wrong, and she flattens it under her heel and makes a new one.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 3)
    You hold a crouch behind the water barrel while the rest of the line goes about its business around you, and Kestrel walks past twice without appearing to look. The third time she stops, studies you for a long moment, and moves on with the smallest nod.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 4)
    A pebble cracks against the fence rail somewhere behind the line, and every Scout drops flat before the sound has finished. Kestrel throws it again from a different spot each time, until dropping stops being a decision and starts being a reflex.
  *else
    Pairs work close with wooden practice knives, low and fast, nothing like the open bouts at the posts. Kestrel corrects a grip here, a stance there, without raising her voice above the scrape of wood on wood.

  *if ((training_sessions = level_up_sessions_needed) and (level_up_sessions_needed > 0))
    When the group breaks for the day, Kestrel catches your eye across the yard, gives a single short nod, and turns back to the posts.
*elseif (squad = "cadre")
  *if (training_sessions = 1)
    Ysolde glances at your hands before your face, decides something from them, and directs you to an open space at the end of the line with two fingers.

  *if (dr_pick = 0)
    Ysolde sets a lit candle in front of you and has you hold the flame steady between two fingers without touching the wick. Every flicker earns a mark on her slate and nothing more, until the candle has burned down by a full inch.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 1)
    You recite a column of figures from Ysolde's ledger while a Cadre recruit beside you knocks a spoon against a tin cup without warning or rhythm. Losing the count means starting the column again from the top.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 2)
    You hold a small working steady while the recruit paired with you does everything short of touching you to break it: a shout, a shove at the shoulder, a hand waved close to your eyes. Ysolde watches both of you and marks only the one who breaks first.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 3)
    Ysolde hands you a warded sigil copied onto vellum and has you trace it again on a fresh sheet, line for line, stroke for stroke. A crooked line earns a fresh sheet and no comment. A clean one earns the next sigil in the stack.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 4)
    The same small cantrip, over and over, until your hands have stopped shaking from the cold and started shaking from the repetition instead. Ysolde does not tell you when to stop. She tells you when you may.
  *else
    Ysolde has you hold a working while walking an ordinary lap of the yard, exactly as if you were carrying nothing at all. A corporal you pass and greet must have no idea. Ysolde falls in beside you afterward and asks only whether he did.

  *if ((training_sessions = level_up_sessions_needed) and (level_up_sessions_needed > 0))
    When the line is dismissed, Ysolde marks something final on her slate, glances at you once, and closes the cover over it.
*else
  *if (training_sessions = 1)
    A corporal with a notched pine tally-board runs a finger down it, finds a gap, and jerks his chin at the end of the nearest line. You fall in.

  *if (dr_pick = 0)
    A drill instructor calls the count for a stance drill, and the recruits bend and straighten together on his word. He lets it run until the ragged voices settle into a single grunt on every step, then makes them do it once more.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 1)
    Pairs spar with wooden practice weapons under a scarred veteran, who ends every bout with a raised fist and points out one thing. It is nearly always the same thing: your feet.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 2)
    The instructors set the recruits running the length of the fence with sand-filled packs. The slowest runners get a second pack on the next lap, and the pace picks up without anyone being told to.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 3)
    A veteran shows a knot of recruits how to fall: shoulder tucked, head off the ground, back on your feet before the fall has finished. Then he makes them do it until it stops being a lesson and becomes a habit.
  *elseif (dr_pick = 4)
    Sacks hang from the practice posts, and the line hits them until their arms shake. The corporals call "Again" whenever a strike lands soft, and it is a long time before anyone gets to stop.
  *else
    Two files form and pass heavy sacks of sand from hand to hand down the length of the yard, then back the other way, while an instructor walks alongside and counts the ones that hit the ground.

  *if ((training_sessions = level_up_sessions_needed) and (level_up_sessions_needed > 0))
    When the group is dismissed the corporal catches your eye across the yard and taps the tally-board twice. That is the last entry on it, and he draws a stroke through it.

*line_break
[b][🎯 Drill | ${training_hours} hours | Training ${training_sessions} / ${level_up_sessions_needed} | ${clock_time}][/b]
*page_break Back across the square…
*goto pv_poi_carrion

*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
*comment YSOLDE'S WORKROOM (pv_poi_workroom): the back room of the timber headquarters, where Vane sends the Cadre for "contract audits
*comment or arcane counsel". A place and a handful of talk topics, no quest: her quests live in scenes/carrion_company/.
*comment Open by day after the morning drill (Pre-Dawn, Night and Late Night shut it, as the hub's pv_is_night does; Morning and Midday she is out on the
*comment drill square, leaders_at_drill, so the hub button leads to pv_workroom_away), and the hub button stays and says so.
*comment Not gated on squad. The hub button only names her once met_ysolde is set, and a first visit by someone who has not met her
*comment sets it when she gives her name. Backstory (see lorebook entry "ysolde"): each fact is one flag and one gated codex paragraph.
*comment   ysolde_waterways_told  respect >= 10: she measured river depths for the Empire's waterways service, aged 18-24.
*comment   ysolde_ostrand_told    respect >= 25 and the first told: she is of House Ostrand and left over keeping her river charts private.
*comment The brass signet lies in the room from the first visit and is only ask-about-able once she has told the first fact.
*comment Her niece or nephew in the house, who runs it now and whether it knows where she is are NOT written: they wait for a quest.
*comment The Rotten Rib (Iron Wharves) starts and ends here. Vane tells every player to see her about the held-up timber orders
*comment (vane_timber_hint, set in port_valen_vane_summons); "Tell her Vane sent you" (rotten_rib_quest_stage unstarted,
*comment ysolde_timber_briefed) sends the player to Slipway Two; once the quest is resolved, "Lay the stamped waybill on her table"
*comment (has_diverted_timber_waybill, ysolde_timber_turned_in) takes the paper back: ysolde_respect +5 on either ending, 5 silver only
*comment on "blackmail" (she pays the day the orders move, and they move only if the oak goes back to Brant).
*comment ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

*label pv_poi_workroom
*looplimit 100000
*set current_district "compound"
*set pv_workroom_seen true
*gosub_scene calendar update_hub_condition
[b]◈ THE WORKROOM[/b]
*line_break
[b][⏳ Port Valen Hour: ${clock_time} (${time_period}) | Hit Points: ${hp_current}/${hp_max}${hub_condition_str}][/b]

A stair of bare planks runs up the outside wall of the timber headquarters, past the window of the contract office, to a plain door with a blank brass plate. Inside, one long table stands under a row of shuttered windows. Charts of the Grey River from the weir to the bay are pinned edge to edge along both walls, and a rack by the door holds coils of thin rope with lead weights knotted along them. Notebooks fill the shelves on the far wall, each spine labelled with a year in a small, even hand. A stove ticks in the corner, and a brass signet lies face-down beside the inkstand.

*if (weather_wet)
  Rain ticks on the shingles over the table.

*if (met_ysolde)
  Ysolde stands at the table with an iron pointer held over a chart, her silver-framed spectacles low on her nose. At a desk by the stove, an elven scribe copies a column of figures without looking up.
*else
  An older woman stands at the table with an iron pointer held over a chart, silver-framed spectacles low on her nose. Dark hair streaked iron-grey is drawn back in braids around two small horns of polished black stone. At a desk by the stove, an elven scribe copies a column of figures without looking up.

A Cadre recruit waits by the door with a slate under one arm, and doesn't move until Ysolde gives her a nod.

*if (squad = "cadre")
  *comment Her own recruit hears the greeting once; on later visits the room just carries on.
  *if (not(ysolde_cadre_greeted))
    *set ysolde_cadre_greeted true
    She glances at your hands before your face. "Shut the door, ${name}."
*elseif (not(met_ysolde))
  She looks up over the spectacles and weighs you the way a customs clerk weighs a crate. "Ysolde Ostrand, handler of the Cadre. If you're bleeding, the infirmary's across the yard. If you're only curious, the charts aren't entertainment, though people keep treating them that way."
  *set met_ysolde true
  *set ysolde_workroom_greeted true
*elseif (not(ysolde_workroom_greeted))
  *set ysolde_workroom_greeted true
  She looks up over the spectacles. "Company business? Infirmary's the other side of the yard, and it's got more patience than I have."

*goto pv_workroom_menu

*label pv_workroom_back
[b]◈ THE WORKROOM[/b]
*line_break
The pointer goes back to the chart, and the scribe's nib keeps scratching.

*label pv_workroom_menu
*choice
  *if ((not(ysolde_waterways_told)) and ((ysolde_respect >= 10) or (not(ysolde_charts_asked))))
    # Ask about the charts on the walls.

*label pv_workroom_charts
[b]◈ THE WORKROOM[/b]
*line_break
*if (ysolde_respect < 10)
  *set ysolde_charts_asked true
  Ysolde doesn't look up from her pointer. "River charts. Weir to bay, every shallow and sandbar I could find, and a few places where a loaded barge sits down and doesn't get up." She marks something. "If you've seen a shallow I haven't, bring me a number. 'About' I can get from any bargeman."
*else
  Ysolde sets the pointer down and walks the length of the wall with you. The pale charts on the left are older than the rest, the ink gone brown at the edges.

  "Those pale ones are the Empire's," she says. "I copied them out before the garrison left, because I had a feeling it would take the originals with it. It did."

  She stops where the handwriting changes. "Everything from the first bend below the weir down to the bay is mine. I did it from a little boat, three summers running. You drop a weighted line over the side and read off how deep it is, and you do that for miles. I wrote, and a boy called the numbers. He was eleven and never got one wrong. I haven't met a grown man since who could say that."

  She takes her spectacles off and taps the blank patch above it. "That's the stretch under the boom. It was the garrison's water, so I never got to chart it, and when they left they took the plans for it too. I watched the cart go. I was twenty-four, and it never crossed my mind that anyone could just drive a river's worth of knowledge away in a cart." The spectacles go back on. "The river's shifted since, of course. They do. I redraw a bend whenever someone brings me a good number."
  *set ysolde_waterways_told true
*page_break Back to the table…
*goto pv_workroom_back

*label pv_workroom_signet
[b]◈ THE WORKROOM[/b]
*line_break
Ysolde turns the signet over with one finger. A brass key crossed with a quill is cut into its face.

"You've probably seen that on a gate up on the Avenue of Estates," she says. "Black iron, brass key, a quill laid across it. That's House Ostrand. It was my family's mark, and I suppose it's still mine, but I don't use it."

She sets it face-down again, exactly where it was.

"The house insures ships. If one sinks, the house pays the owner, so it cares a great deal about where ships are likely to sink. For eight years after the Empire left, the best answer to that on the Grey River was my charts." She moves the inkstand a finger's width. "There's a rule in my family: never insure a danger you can't measure, and never share a measurement you can sell. I thought that was wrong. I wanted the charts posted on the customs tower where any skipper could read them. Fewer wrecks, for everyone. The house asked what it would get out of that. Nothing, I said. They said that was exactly the problem. If a thing's free to everybody, nobody will pay you for it."

"We didn't agree, and by then Captain Vane had offered me work. He puts his terms in writing and he pays on the day. Twenty-two years of it now. You'd be amazed how rare that is up on the Avenue." One corner of her mouth lifts. "When I swear, it's usually 'perils of the river.' That's from the house's insurance papers. It's what they write when a ship's lost and nobody's to blame, so nobody pays. Old habit."

The pointer comes up again. "Ruler. By your elbow, there. Thanks."
*set ysolde_ostrand_told true
*page_break Back to the table…
*goto pv_workroom_back

*label pv_workroom_timber
[b]◈ THE WORKROOM[/b]
*line_break
*set ysolde_timber_briefed true
Ysolde sets the pointer down and looks at you over the spectacles for a moment longer than usual.

"Vane sent you about the orders, then. Good, I asked him to." She taps the table. "Eleven days, and the Scales' dock clerks still say they've no barge space to spare. Odd, because last Marketday the Osprey, one of their own barges, left the Iron Wharves sheds riding eight inches lower than she came in." The scribe's nib doesn't pause. "Something heavy went onto her, and heavy things going downriver come with a paper that explains that usually list the manifest."

She taps the pointer once on the table. "I want that paper. Not the clerk, not the cargo, the paper, with its seal whole. Whoever's name is on it can't call it an accident. If the timber ends up back where it belongs, the Scales' barge excuse is dead and the orders move, and I'll pay five silver the day they do."

"The risk is yours," she adds. "If the yard hears the Cadre sent you, the orders stay stuck for good. There's a shipwright on Slipway Two losing money by the tide, and men who lose money talk, so start there. And shut that door properly on your way out."
*page_break Back to the table…
*goto pv_workroom_back

*label pv_workroom_timber_turnin
[b]◈ THE WORKROOM[/b]
*line_break
You unfold the waybill on the table and lay it flat under her pointer. The red wax of the factor's personal stamp has a clean edge all the way round.

Ysolde doesn't touch it. She tilts her head and reads it where it lies, then lifts one corner with the tip of the pointer and checks the wax underneath.

"Hendryk's own stamp. Not the yard clerk's mark, the factor's." She lets the corner fall. "Somebody put the factor's name on a lie, and he doesn't know yet. That's worth more than the oak."

*if (rotten_rib_resolution = "blackmail")
  "The oak's back on Brant's slip," she says. "So the Scales' barge excuse died this morning, and our quartermaster's orders moved at first bell." She opens a drawer, lays the waybill in it, and slides a small stack of silver marks across the table. "Five, as I said."
*else
  "The ship went out on green wood," she says. "Thirty people I can't put a number on." She is quiet for a moment. "The orders are still stuck, so there's nothing to pay for yet. But a paper with that stamp on it is worth keeping, and now somebody's keeping it."
  She opens a drawer and lays the waybill inside.

*if (not(ysolde_timber_turned_in))
  *set ysolde_timber_turned_in true
  *set has_diverted_timber_waybill false

*label pv_poi_armory
*looplimit 100000
*set current_district "compound"
*set met_varren true
*gosub_scene calendar update_hub_condition
[b]◈ THE ARMORY SHED[/b]
*line_break
[b][⏳ Port Valen Hour: ${clock_time} (${time_period}) | Hit Points: ${hp_current}/${hp_max}${hub_condition_str}][/b]

A long timber shed stands against the seawall with its double doors propped open on iron stays. Inside it is cold and smells of oil and whetstone dust. Spears stand in racks along one wall, mail shirts hang from pegs along the other, and a brine trough by the door holds an axe-head wrapped in rag. At the far end an apprentice armorer works a foot-driven grindstone behind a plank partition, and beside it a bench is covered with strap leather, buckles and a half-mended shield rim.

*if (weather_wet)
  Rain ticks on the shingles over the racks.

A corporal stands just inside the door with a notched tally-board, ticking off soldiers as they draw spare straps and buckles from the armorer. He looks you over once, then jerks his chin toward the bench at the far end.

Sergeant Varren sits at the bench with the shield rim across his knees, working a new strap through the rivets with thick fingers. He doesn't look up when you come in.

*comment The greeting plays on the first visit only; later visits go straight to the bench.
*if (not(varren_armory_greeted))
  *set varren_armory_greeted true
  *if (varren_respect < 6)
    "Well?" he says, to the rivet.
  *elseif (squad = "vanguard")
    "Shut the door behind you," he says. "You're letting my heat out."
  *else
    "You've got a question," he says. "I can tell by how you're standing."

*goto pv_armory_menu

*label pv_armory_back
[b]◈ THE ARMORY SHED[/b]
*line_break
The strap goes back through the rivet, and the grindstone behind the partition starts up again.

*label pv_armory_menu
*choice
  *if (not(varren_advice_told))
    # Ask what he'd tell a recruit who wants to live.
      *goto pv_armory_advice
  *if (not(varren_alderford_told))
    # Ask what he made of the work you did in Alderford.
      *goto pv_armory_alderford
  *if ((varren_respect >= 10) and (not(varren_redfort_told)))
    # Ask about Redfort.
      *goto pv_armory_redfort
  # Head back across the compound.
    *goto port_valen_hub

*label pv_armory_advice
[b]◈ THE ARMORY SHED[/b]
*line_break
*set varren_advice_told true

Varren braces the shield between his knees and hauls the new strap through to the last rivet. He leans his weight against it once, then sets the shield against the bench.

*if (visited_varren)
  "You visited me before the causeway." He reaches for another length of leather. "Remember what you asked me?"

  "About the ground."

  "Aye. Everyone else wanted to know if we'd win." He jerks his chin toward the stool opposite. "Sit."
*else
  "I didn't see you before the causeway." He says it without accusation, measuring a strip of leather against his forearm. "Men like hearing they're brave more than they like hearing how to stay alive." He jerks his chin toward the stool opposite. "Sit. Might as well hear it now."

The shed smells of lamp oil, wet wool, and the vinegar Varren uses to clean rust from neglected mail. Somewhere beyond the partition, a hammer rings twice against an anvil.

"Four things." He holds up a scarred finger. "Keep your feet dry. You'd be amazed how many good soldiers I've watched lose a march to bad boots."

A second finger. "Keep your iron clean. Rust doesn't care that you're tired." He taps the strap across his knee. "And learn to mend it yourself. A strap that parts in the field won't wait for the armorer."

A third. "Stay with your file. If you can reach the man beside you, he can reach you."

The fourth stays raised a moment. "And mind your open side."

You wait.

Varren lowers his hand. "That's the one that kills you."

*if (squad = "vanguard")
  He picks up the shield again and runs his thumb beneath the new strap. "You're Vanguard now. When the shields close, don't stare at the bastard in front of you. You know where he is. Know where the man beside you is. Keep the gap shut."

  "And trust the other end of the line?"

  "You'd better. Can't hold both ends yourself."
*else
  He picks up the shield again and runs his thumb beneath the new strap. "You're not Vanguard. Doesn't matter. Sooner or later you'll be standing beside us. When that happens, don't make the man next to you wonder where you've gone."

He reaches for the next shield.

"That's it?"

"That's it."

"Four things."

"Four things." He bends over the next buckle. "Everything else is just finding more expensive ways to learn them."

*page_break Back to the bench…
*goto pv_armory_back


*label pv_armory_alderford
[b]◈ THE ARMORY SHED[/b]
*line_break
*set varren_alderford_told true
*temp av_count 0

Varren threads a fresh strap through a shield grip, works it back and forth, then gives it a hard pull.

"I hear things," he says, reaching for the awl. "Sergeant who doesn't hear things usually ends up surprised. I don't like surprises."

*if ((maura_cellar_cleared) and (av_count < 3))
  *set av_count +1
  The awl stops against the leather. "Maura's cellar. Heard about that."

  "People talk."

  "People always talk." He punches the awl through and twists it free. "No light. No room to form up. Can't see what's behind you, can't get a shield around what's in front of you." He shakes his head. "Give me rain and an open field any day."

  "Thought you hated open ground."

  "I do." He reaches for the next rivet. "That's how much I hate cellars."

*if (av_count = 0)
  "Alderford, not much," he admits. "You kept your boots dry and your head down."

  *if (spared_gate_squatter)
    He works the awl in slowly. "The gate wall at the Sinks, though. You let that deserter run. I'd have put her off the wall myself." He pulls the strap tight. "Might have been wrong. Don't tell the others I said so."
  *else
    He works the awl in slowly. "The gate wall at the Sinks, though. Whatever you did with that deserter, you did it fast, and the wall stayed ours." He pulls the strap tight. "That's all a sergeant needs from a soldier."

*page_break Back to the bench…
*goto pv_armory_back


*label pv_armory_redfort
[b]◈ THE ARMORY SHED[/b]
*line_break
*set varren_redfort_told true

Varren is halfway through setting a rivet when you mention Redfort.

The hammer doesn't fall. For a few seconds there is only the hiss of the brazier and the scrape of someone sorting spearheads behind the partition.

"Redfort." He sets the hammer down. "Winter siege. Three months."

His milk-pale eye stays on the shield between his knees. "Couldn't dig deep because the ground froze. Couldn't light enough fires because they'd range the engines on the smoke. By the end we were boiling harness leather."

"The Baron's steward haggled the bounty down the whole time," he adds. "Vane paid the unit out of the company chest anyway. The Baron's coin came three months late."

He rubs a thumb over the unfinished rivet. "I drilled three units for that siege. Forty in the one I marched in myself. Eleven came out."

You say nothing. Somewhere behind the partition, a hammer starts up, then falters.

Varren reaches for his own hammer again. "You want their names?"

"Do you want to tell me?"

"No." The hammer comes down once. "I remember them. That's enough."

Another strike seats the rivet. "The next file gets drilled harder. Then the next one harder than that." He turns the shield over and checks his work. "That's what I do with the dead."

For a moment, neither of you speaks. Then he pushes the finished shield toward the rack with his boot.

"Hand me that buckle."

You pass it to him.

"Good." He takes it and reaches for the next shield. "Now we're doing something useful."

*page_break Back to the bench…
*goto pv_armory_back

*label pv_armory_shut
[b]◈ THE ARMORY SHED[/b]
*line_break
The double doors of the armory shed are chained, and a sentry under the lantern shakes his head before you reach them. "Shut for the night," he says. "The sergeant drills the Vanguard on the square from first light. The shed opens when the drill's done, and then it's open afternoons and evenings."
*page_break Back across the compound…
*goto port_valen_hub

*label pv_poi_tower

[b]◈ THE WATCHTOWER[/b]
*line_break
[b][⏳ Port Valen Hour: ${clock_time} (${time_period}) | Hit Points: ${hp_current}/${hp_max}${hub_condition_str}][/b]

A sentry in soot-dark mail stands at the foot of the tower ladder with a poleaxe grounded beside him. He looks at your face, not your badge, and steps aside. "You've been cleared," he says, to nobody in particular. "Up you go. Mind your step."

The ladder comes up through a trapdoor into a small timber room under the roof. The shutters are open on all four sides, and the wind comes straight through them with a cold smell of tar and low tide. A rack by the trapdoor holds a row of signal lanterns with their hoods off. A bench has been worn smooth by years of sitting. On an upturned crate in the corner, someone has scratched a map of the inland road in charcoal, and half of it has already been rubbed away.

*if (weather = "Fog")
  Fog has filled the shutters, and the yard below has gone to a murmur of voices and nothing else.
*elseif (weather_wet)
  Rain slants in under the eaves and beads on the lantern rack.
*elseif (time_period = "Dusk")
  The last light lies along the seawall, and the braziers in the yard are being lit one by one.
*else
  Beyond the shutters the yard is a scatter of braziers, and past the wall the harbor lights shiver on the water.

A scout sits against the wall beside the trapdoor with a cased bow across her knees. She has watched every step of your climb, and she doesn't stop now.

Kestrel sits in the shutter that faces the inland road, one boot up on the sill, looking at something you can't see. She doesn't turn around.

*goto pv_tower_menu

*label pv_tower_back
[b]◈ THE WATCHTOWER[/b]
*line_break
The wind rubs a little more of the charcoal off the crate, and the scout by the trapdoor goes back to watching the ladder.

  *if ((silt_gate_quest_stage = "unstarted") and (not(kestrel_silt_briefed)))

  *if ((has_silt_gate_payout_slip) and (not(kestrel_slip_turned_in)))
    # Lay the broker's payout slip on the bench.
      *goto pv_tower_silt_turnin
  *if (not(kestrel_advice_told))
    # Ask what she sees from up here.
      *goto pv_tower_advice
  *if (not(kestrel_sinks_told))
    # Ask what she made of the Sinks and the road to Alderford.
      *goto pv_tower_sinks
  *if ((kestrel_respect >= 6) and (not(kestrel_origin_told)))
    # Ask where she's from.

  # Climb back down.

*label pv_tower_advice
[b]◈ THE WATCHTOWER[/b]
*line_break
*set kestrel_advice_told true
*if (squad = "scouts")
  *temp kt_base kestrel_respect
  *if (kestrel_slip_turned_in)
    *set kt_base (kestrel_respect - 5)
  *if (kt_base >= 12)
    "${name}," Kestrel says, not turning. "I gave your name to the Captain, since you've climbed to the top of my list of new recruits I actually like. He wants secret agents."
  *elseif (kt_base >= 6)
    "${name}," Kestrel says, not turning. "You've kept your feet quiet, which is more than most manage. The Captain wants secret operatives. I'm one of the people that gave him your name."
  *elseif (kt_base >= 0)
    "${name}," Kestrel says, not turning. "You haven't let me down, and you haven't impressed me either. We'll see if that changes."
  *else
    "${name}," Kestrel says, flat, not turning. "You killed that woman on the gate wall. I said messy, and I haven't changed my mind." She lets a moment pass. "You're one of mine regardless. The Captain asked who I'd send, and everyone else in my unit I actually like."
*elseif (kestrel_skiff = "light_feet")
  "${name}." Kestrel says it as if testing the weight of it, not turning. "Light feet, at the skiff. In that fog I thought I'd be sending someone to fish you out of the reeds. You impressed me. The Captain wants hands with a sharp mind and initiative, so I said your name."
*elseif (kestrel_skiff = "good_questions")
  "${name}," Kestrel says, not turning. "Asking good questions is a good way to stay alive. The Captain wants hands that aren't on his roster. You had a good question and a sharp mind, so I said your name."
*elseif (kestrel_skiff = "delivered")
  "${name}, right?" Kestrel says, not turning. "I remembered you. You left the bodkins on the skiff and went off without a word."
*elseif (kestrel_skiff = "stumbled")
  "${name}," Kestrel says, not turning. "The root. Loudest thing in the Sinks that night, and that's counting the gatehouse. I nearly put an arrow through you." She shrugs. "You're still here. I'll let it go."
*elseif (kestrel_skiff = "brushed_off")
  "${name}," Kestrel says, not turning. "I was short with you at the skiff. It had been a long night and a longer fog." She shrugs. "Come in, then. I've nothing against people who ask."
*elseif (spared_gate_squatter)
  "${name}," Kestrel says, not turning. "I know your name. I know everyone's, it's the job. We've never talked, but I know your face. You let that woman go on the gate wall, and a dozen people came out of the reeds behind her. I noticed that, and I told Vane so when he asked."
*else
  "${name}," Kestrel says, not turning. "I know your name. I know everyone's, it's the job. We've never talked, but I know your face."

She hooks a boot round the stool and drags it a few inches along the boards. "Over there. Not that shutter, you'll block the road."

You crouch at the next shutter. The compound lies below you, small and busy: carts, a soldier shouting at nobody, smoke going sideways off the cookhouse roof.

"The sentry on the wall-walk," she says. "What's he doing?"

You look. "Nothing."

"For how long?"

You look for longer. "Since we sat down."

"Since before that." She scratches her ear. "He's either asleep on his feet or waiting for someone. When you watch someone for long enough, hours, days, months and years, you begin to pick up and learn things that even they don't know about themselves."

"That sounds exhausting."

"It is. I'm told I'm terrible company. I stare too much. I'd get used to staring at people at odd hours, if you'll be working with me."

*page_break Back to the shutter…
*goto pv_tower_back

*label pv_tower_sinks
[b]◈ THE WATCHTOWER[/b]
*line_break
*set kestrel_sinks_told true
*temp kt_count 0
Kestrel takes a strip of dried eel out of her belt, bites off half, and holds the rest out to you without looking round. You take it. It's like chewing a boot.

"I hear things," she says. "Mostly because I'm sat up here with nothing else to do."

*if (spared_gate_squatter)
  *set kt_count +1
  "That woman on the gate wall. You let her go." She nods slowly. "I wasn't sure you would. A dozen of them came out of the reeds behind her before you'd gone twenty yards. Saved my people a very wet morning."

*if ((maura_cellar_cleared) and (kt_count < 3))
  *set kt_count +1
  "I had two lads on the roof across from Maura's when you went down that cellar. They had a bet on whether you'd come back up."

*if (((rennick_disposition = "intimidated") or (rennick_disposition = "paid_off")) and (kt_count < 3))
  *set kt_count +1
  "And the bailiff at Talia's loft. I heard you never raised your voice." She looks at the road. "People hear that and think it was easy."
*elseif ((rennick_disposition = "humiliated") and (kt_count < 3))
  *set kt_count +1
  "And the bailiff at Talia's loft. I heard your fists did the talking." She chews. "He'll be careful of anyone in our colors now."
*elseif ((rennick_disposition = "hostile") and (kt_count < 3))
  *set kt_count +1
  "And the bailiff at Talia's loft. You left him angry. He's the sort who doesn't forget." She turns the eel over in her fingers. "I'd watch your back if you ever return to Alderford."

*if ((alder_river_chain_cleared) and (kt_count < 3))
  *set kt_count +1
  "The chains in the river, too. My people walked that bank two days and never saw it." She grunts. "Ysolde found it from a boat with a length of rope. As much she and I are friends, I do hate when she upstages me."

*if (kt_count = 0)
  "Honestly, I haven't much on you," she admits. "Keep your head down. It works more often than you'd think."
*else
  "That's what I've got," she says, and brushes the crumbs off her knee. "I'd stop there if I were you. I've no more eel."

*page_break Back to the shutter…
*goto pv_tower_back

*label pv_tower_origin
[b]◈ THE WATCHTOWER[/b]
*line_break
*set kestrel_origin_told true
Kestrel doesn't answer for a while. The scout by the trapdoor shifts and finds something very interesting about her bow.

"Fen," she says at last. "East of the causeway, and then a long way further. You wouldn't find it, and you wouldn't like it if you did." She tips her head at the dark past the inland road. "Houses on stilts. In the spring the water comes up through the floor and you sleep on the table."

"Who with?"

"My mother. She poled eels to market."

"And your father?"

"Passed through. An elf, with one of those survey parties that the Empire sent out, all brass and measuring." She says it the way you'd mention the weather. "He left my mother pregnant and left before she even knew she was pregnant."

She picks at the crate with a thumbnail, and the charcoal comes away in little black flakes. "Half-elf in a place that small. Everyone looked and nobody spoke. So I was on the water a lot. Turns out that's a job, if you know which reeds will hold a boat."

"That's how I ended up in the Empire's Legion at Gryke. An old fort. I was fifteen." Her mouth twitches. "They'd lost two patrols in the mire and were short of people who weren't drowned. Pay was bad and the food was worse. I'd never been so well off."

"Is Kestrel really your name?"

"It's what they called me. I used to stand at the edge of open water for an age, looking, before I put a foot in. My commander at the time said I looked like a bird over a ditch." She almost smiles. "The name Kestrel stuck."

"And the other one?"

"Stays where I left it." She turns back to the road. "Don't take it personally."

*goto pv_tower_back

*label pv_tower_silt
[b]◈ THE WATCHTOWER[/b]
*line_break
*set kestrel_silt_briefed true
Kestrel turns from the shutter for the first time since you climbed up, and looks you over as if you were a stretch of road.

"Yes, what Vane sent you for." It isn't a question. She jerks her head at the stool.

"Down on the quay, by the customs tower, the dockmaster has an issue the Watch is either too busy or too corrupt to solve. He's a lovely fellow, shouts at clerks. He's got smugglers coming in through the drain under Dredge-End at high tide, steel and spirits, and he wants them stopped. I think the Watch has the right idea here to ignore it. It would be like trying to kiss the wind, attempting to stop the smuggling of alcohol." She picks a splinter off the sill.

"And you want me to look into this? To kiss the wind? What do you want out of this?"

"Yes, take the job, certainly. He'll talk to you, and nobody asks why a stranger's skulking round the canals. Just need you to do it once. It'll probably drop the illegal, untaxed supply of it for a day or two." She looks at you properly. "I want the people who are making this possible in our pockets. The Watch is corrupt and no doubt is accepting bribes. Find who these people are. Just don't hand him anything. He'd do the decent thing with it, and I'd rather he didn't."

"And what do I get?"

"Five silver if you bring names."

*goto pv_tower_back

*label pv_tower_silt_turnin
[b]◈ THE WATCHTOWER[/b]
*line_break
You unfold the strip of wax vellum and lay it on the bench between you.

Kestrel doesn't touch it at first. She holds it to the lantern and reads it, lips moving a little, then rubs her thumb across the wax to feel where it was pressed.

"Kray," she says. "And Pell. I'd have put money on Pell." She breathes out through her nose. "Six marks a Marketday, the pair of them. Cheaper than I'd feared."

She folds the vellum into her coat and counts five silver marks onto the bench. "As agreed."

*if (silt_gate_face_seen)

  You tell her about the porters and the lanterns.

  "Then keep clear of Dredge-End for a few days." She shrugs. "Faces fade. The names on this won't."
*else
  "Neither of them saw you take it?"

  You tell her they didn't.

  "Good. Keep it that way."

*label port_valen_rest
You unbuckle your harness, strip off your sweat-stiffened gambeson, and drop your pack beside your straw pallet.

*if (squad = "vanguard")
  Your bunk sits between shield racks and a row of drying boots in the long barracks. The room smells of tallow, oiled mail, and damp wool. On the far side of the room, off-duty veterans trade low grunts over a greasy deck of cards while a corporal trims a candle wick.
*elseif (squad = "scouts")
  Your cot is tucked under the low pitch of the stores loft. Unstrung recurve bows hang from pegs overhead, clear of the damp floor, while the faint smell of cedar shavings and fletching glue hangs in the warm rafters.
*elseif (squad = "cadre")
  Your bedroll is laid out behind a canvas partition under the contract office. Staffs rest in a stout corner rack, and the air smells of lamp oil, drying ink, and boiled marsh-tinctures.
*else
  *comment squad = "none" / independent recruit
  Your assigned straw paillasse sits in the recruit overflow shed by the wagon bays, wedged between grain sacks and spare harness gear. Around you, new recruits nurse blisters in the drafty quiet.

*if (river_crossing_rattled)
  The dark bruise from the river crossing throbs against your hip as you stretch out on the straw.
*if (river_crossing_weary)
  The damp cold from the river gorge still clings to your fingers; you tuck your hands under your arms and wait for warmth to return.

The gravel yard outside keeps its steady rhythm: the thud of a sentry's hobnails on the catwalk, a mule shifting in the stables, the low murmur of the night watch changing posts. You close your eyes, and the compound noise fades into the dark.

*label port_valen_vane_summons
*set met_vane true
*temp vq_raven false
*temp vq_watch false
[b]◈ THE CONTRACT OFFICE[/b]
*line_break

*if (squad = "vanguard")
  Varren is already at the foot of your pallet when the bells stop ringing, arms crossed, watching you lace your boots. "Move. Captain wants you, and he doesn't like waiting on recruits."
*if (squad = "scouts")
  Kestrel crouches by your pallet, quiet enough that you don't hear her arrive until she speaks. "Vane's asking for you," she says. "Don't make him ask twice."
*if (squad = "cadre")
  Ysolde raps her knuckles against the canvas screen. "The captain wants you at headquarters. Now, not after you've eaten."

*if (squad = "vanguard")
  Varren leads you through the yard without another word, boots cutting straight through the mud past the ranks already forming for the day.
*if (squad = "scouts")
  Kestrel takes the long way around the drying racks, the same unhurried pace she uses for everything, and leaves you at his door without explanation.
*if (squad = "cadre")
  Ysolde walks beside you as far as the heavy doorway, then peels off toward her own morning paperwork without breaking stride.

Vane's study is smaller than the compound outside makes it look: a trestle desk crowded with muster rolls and transit vouchers, one shuttered window letting in a blade of grey morning light, and the company's raven banner nailed flat to the masonry behind him instead of hung loose from a pole. He finishes the line he's writing before he looks up.

"Sit, or don't." He shuts the ledger he was reading and squares it against the desk edge. "Two hundred of us marched out of Valen. Four hundred came back. Half the new ones have never stood in a shield wall, and when the Watch asks who answers for the mess, they get my name."

He settles back in his chair, cold grey eyes measuring you from boots to collar.

*if (vane_standing > 0)
  "Alderford. You came into my own tent with a boom hazard in your hands. A sergeant would've sat on that for a week and put four barges on the riverbed." He turns a page. "I remember the ones who bring me problems already solved."
*elseif (alder_river_chain_cleared)
  "Ysolde's report from Alderford had your name in it. A boom hazard on the Grey River, pulled before it took four barges down." He taps the sheet in front of him. "I read everything that crosses this desk. Yours stuck."

*temp vs_named false
*if ((rennick_disposition = "intimidated") or (rennick_disposition = "paid_off"))
  *set vs_named true
  "And Varren says you moved a Gilded Scales bailiff off Talia's curing loft without drawn steel." He sets the quill down. "Fights cost me coin and widows. That sort of work costs me neither, and the whole waterfront heard about it. I'd take ten of those over a dozen pitched fights."
*elseif (rennick_disposition = "humiliated")
  *set vs_named true
  "Varren mentioned a bailiff who thought he could skim Talia's tolls until your fists talked him out of it. Untidy. But nothing reached the Watch's ledgers, and I can live with untidy."
*elseif (carries_chapel_grain)
  *set vs_named true
  "Overseer Bran said you were the recruit who hauled his condemned grain up to Saint Althea's on your own time. No orders. Nothing in it for you." A short pause. "People remember that far longer than they remember a battle."
*elseif (maura_cellar_cleared)
  *set vs_named true
  "Kestrel's runners say you cleared out whatever monster Maura has in her cellar. I'm sure it's still rotting there. That's the sort of initiative I like."

*if ((has_hearthstone_talisman) and (janna_pins_forged))
  *set vs_named true
  "Torvald's mill-wheel was one bad night from tearing its own axle out, and sixteen mooring pins were due at the factor's dock before dawn muster." He shrugs one shoulder. "Varren says you were on the platform for the one and swinging a sledge for the other. Half the company's edges are ground at that smithy. I'd rather it owed us."
*elseif (has_hearthstone_talisman)
  *set vs_named true
  "Torvald's mill-wheel was one bad night from tearing its own axle out, and half the company's edges are ground on that stone." He shrugs one shoulder. "Varren says you were on the platform when it came free. A smith who owes us a favor is worth more to me than one who doesn't."
*elseif (janna_pins_forged)
  *set vs_named true
  "Ysolde's tally shows sixteen mooring pins at the factor's dock before dawn muster." He taps the sheet. "The journeyman who forged them says she had a striker nobody assigned to her. Half the company's edges are ground at that smithy, and I'd rather it owed us."

*if (not(vs_named))
  "Varren, Kestrel, and Ysolde have all put your name in front of me since the causeway, and not one of them complained about you."

"That's why I'm asking you and not the next recruit." He leans forward and rests his forearms on the desk. "I need hands that aren't on my roster. No raven badge, no company colors. To every dock-boss and factor in this city, you're a hired hand looking for street work. That's the whole point. Four hundred of us in raven livery would turn a scuffle into a riot."

"The Council holds the charter and the purse, and the Scales hold the Council. The Watch holds the street, thinly. The Black Oath holds the canals." He counts them off on three blunt fingers. "Shake whatever hand you have to. I only care that the Carrion holds the leash when you wash up."

*if (squad = "vanguard")
  "You're off the muster roll, not off Varren's. If you want a second opinion on a fight, or gear from the company stores, find him in the armory shed."
*if (squad = "scouts")
  "You're off the muster roll, not off Kestrel's. If you need routes or word from the streets, find her in the watchtower."
*if (squad = "cadre")
  "You're off the muster roll, not off Ysolde's. Contract audits or arcane counsel, go to her workroom."

*set vane_timber_hint true
"Whatever your squad, see Ysolde this week," Vane adds. "The Scales have held our timber and forage orders for eleven days and say they've no barge space. She says the figures don't fit. Every clerk on those wharves knows a raven badge when it walks up, and you're not wearing one."

"And go up the watchtower after dark," Vane adds. "Kestrel keeps late hours, and she has a use for someone who isn't on my roster."

"Two rules," Vane adds. "The first is the captain's, the one she read out at the pier: no private collections, no unsanctioned arrests, no old debts settled under city colors. Our contract with the city pays too well to lose over you. The second is mine. If the Watch takes you, I don't know your name."

"You've got until the contract ends. Work the districts, find me strings I can pull, and you'll be paid for it."

*label port_valen_vane_offer
*choice
  *if (not(vq_raven))
    # "Why hide the raven?"
      *set vq_raven true
      "Because a raven badge opens no doors in this city," Vane says. "Factors bar them, dock-bosses charge double, and every canal runner knows whose leash you're on. A stranger gets talked to. The company gets watched."
      *goto port_valen_vane_offer
  *if (not(vq_watch))
    # "What if the Watch takes me in?"
      *set vq_watch true
      "Then you're a hired hand who broke a city law, and I've never heard of you," Vane says. "The magistrates sit every morning. Be in front of one before the week is out, and keep your name plain."
      *goto port_valen_vane_offer
  # "Understood, Captain. I'll make sure the Carrion gets leverage wherever it counts."
    Vane gives a single nod. "See that you do. My office, every second week. Sooner if something's burning."
  # "Playing all sides between the Watch, the Guilds, and the canals is a quick way to make enemies of all three."
    "It is," Vane says, unbothered. "Rope holds you up or hangs you, depending how you use it. I'm gambling you know the difference. Fortnight. My office."
  # "I can work with that. As long as the coin and the standing follow."
    For once, something like cold approval crosses Vane's face. "Good. Ambition's useful, so long as it doesn't outrun your judgment. Fortnight. My office."

Vane pulls the next dispatch toward him before you've fully turned for the door. As far as he's concerned, the conversation is already finished.
